\overline{g_{\omega}}\in\mathcal{D}(D)
,
so that
\overline{g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f}
is a compactly supported (and hence tempered) distribution on all of

\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which is even a well-defined functional on
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Then, the Paley-Wiener theorem (cf.
[
32
, Theorem 7.23]
) shows that the inverse Fourier transform

\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\overline{g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f})
is given by (integration against)
a smooth function, which is pointwise defined by

(\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\overline{g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f}])(y)=\left\langle\overline{\rule{0.0pt}{7.11317pt}g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f},e^{2\pi i\left\langle y,\cdot\right\rangle}\right\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{\infty})^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\infty}}=\langle\mathcal{F}\psi_{f},\,\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D),\mathcal{D}(D)},

proving Equation (
4.5
).

Finally, to show injectivity of
\Psi
, assume
\psi_{f}\equiv 0
for some
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
.
By Equation (
4.5
), this implies
V_{\theta,\Phi}f\equiv 0
and hence
f=0
,
since
V_{\theta,\Phi}:(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}\to\mathbf{L}_{1/{v}}^{\infty}(\Lambda)
is injective, see ,e.g.,
[
22
, Corollary 2.19]
.
∎

4.2.
The embedding

\Psi:\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})

In Theorem
2.5
, we have noted that
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
is independent of the choice of
\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in
\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)
. Nonetheless, to prove that the embedding
\Psi
given in Corollary
4.3
restricts to an embedding of
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
into
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
, it will be helpful to choose
\theta
appropriately, see Lemma
4.6
below. Before proceeding, we verify that the decomposition space
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
is indeed
well-defined. In fact, Proposition
3.9
has established the existence of a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-BAPU, such that
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
is a semi-structured, admissible decomposition covering of
D
by Lemma

3.2
. It only remains to show is that the weight
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is

\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-moderate, cf. Definition
2.7
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
1\leq q\leq\infty
and let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible.
Then, with
\kappa_{\Phi}=\kappa\circ\Phi^{-1}
, the weight
u=u^{(q)}
, given by

u^{(q)}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\ k\mapsto u_{k}^{(q)}:=\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}},

as in Equation (
4.2
), is
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-moderate.

Proof.

This is a direct application of Lemma
3.2
, Item (2), with
y_{k}=\Phi^{-1}(\delta k)
,
v^{\prime}=\kappa_{\Phi}\cdot w^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}
and
v=\tilde{\kappa}\cdot v_{0}^{\frac{d}{q}+\frac{d}{2}}
. To see this, note that
v_{0}\geq 1
and that any nonnegative power of a submultiplicative weight is itself submultiplicative and
w^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}
is
v_{0}^{|\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}|}
-moderate.
∎

While the decomposition space norm is computed using the Fourier-localized
pieces
\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\varphi_{k}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f}]
,
the coorbit space norm on
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
relies on
the voice transform
V_{\theta,\Phi}f
.
The following lemma establishes a first connection between the two quantities
which will prove essential for showing

\|\psi_{f}\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})}
.
For genuine
\mathbf{L}^{2}
-functions
f
, the lemma is proven by a simple
but lengthy computation; the main technical challenge is the extension to all of

(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible. Fix
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, and
0<\vartheta<r-\sqrt{d}/2
. Finally, choose
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

(4.6)

\theta\geq 0\,,\quad\|\theta\|_{1}=1,\quad\text{ and }\quad K_{0}:=\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)\subset\delta\cdot B_{\vartheta}(0).

Define
\varphi^{(\omega)}:=[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}g_{\omega}
, with
g_{\omega}

as usual, and

M_{k}:=\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left(k+\smash{\left[\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{d}}\vphantom{\left[\frac{1}{2}\right]}\,\right)\right)
, for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Then
(\varphi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
, with

\varphi_{k}:D\to[0,\infty),\eta\mapsto\int_{M_{k}}\varphi^{(\xi)}(\eta)\,d\xi,

is a BAPU for the covering
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
.

Furthermore, for
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
, we have

(4.7)

[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{k}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f})](y)=\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)\,d\omega

for arbitrary
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Proof.

That
(\varphi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is a BAPU for
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.9
: Simply set
\zeta=\theta
in Definition
3.7
. We proceed to prove the second assertion. For arbitrary
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we showed, in the proof of Corollary
4.3
, that
\varphi_{k}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f}
is a (tempered) distribution with compact support.
Hence,

(4.8)

[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{k}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f})](y)=\langle f,\overline{\mathcal{F}[\mathbf{M}_{y}\varphi_{k}]}\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}=(\ast)\,\,,

where Lemma
4.2
shows that
\overline{\mathcal{F}[\mathbf{M}_{y}\varphi_{k}]}\in\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}
, since
\varphi_{k}\in\mathcal{D}(D)
. In particular, this calculation shows that the left-hand side of
the Equation
4.7
depends weak-
\ast
-continuously on
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
.

Next, if
f\in\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)\subset(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
,
we can continue the preceding calculation as follows:

(4.9)

\begin{split}(\ast)&=\left\langle f,\,\overline{\mathcal{F}[\mathbf{M}_{y}\varphi_{k}]}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\left\langle f,\,\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\,\overline{\mathbf{M}_{y}\varphi_{k}}\,]\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\left\langle\widehat{f},\,\overline{\mathbf{M}_{y}\varphi_{k}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\\
&=\int_{D}\widehat{f}(\xi)\cdot\varphi_{k}(\xi)\cdot e^{2\pi i\left\langle y,\xi\right\rangle}\,d\xi\\
({\scriptstyle{\text{Def. of }\varphi_{k}\text{ and Fubini}}})&=\int_{M_{k}}\int_{D}\varphi^{(\omega)}(\xi)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi)\cdot e^{2\pi i\left\langle y,\xi\right\rangle}\,d\xi\,d\omega\\
({\scriptstyle{g_{\omega}\text{ real}}})&=\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\int_{D}\overline{g_{\omega}(\xi)}\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi)\cdot e^{2\pi i\left\langle y,\xi\right\rangle}\,d\xi\,d\omega\\
({\scriptstyle{\text{Plancherel}}})&=\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\left\langle f,\,\mathbf{T}_{y}[\mathcal{F}^{-1}\,g_{\omega}]\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,d\omega\\
&=\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)\,d\omega.\end{split}

This shows Equation (
4.7
), for
f\in\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)\subset(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
. To justify the application of Fubini’s theorem, note the following:
Straightforward calculations using
v_{0}^{d}
-moderateness of
w
(see Theorem
2.5
) show that
[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{1/2}\|\varphi^{(\omega)}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(D)}=\|g_{\omega}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(D)}\leq\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{v_{0}^{d/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{M_{k}}\int_{D}|\varphi^{(\omega)}(\xi)\cdot\widehat{f}(\xi)|\,d\xi\,d\omega

\displaystyle\leq\int_{M_{k}}\|\varphi^{(\omega)}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(D)}\cdot\|\widehat{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(D)}\,d\omega

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\cdot\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{v_{0}^{d/2}}}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\,d\omega<\infty.

Here, we used that the integrand on the right-hand side is continuous and
\overline{M_{k}}
is compact.

It remains to lift the identity
(
4.9
) from
f\in\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)

to arbitrary
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
.
To this end, fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and note that the
set
M_{k}
satisfies

\overline{M_{k}}\subset\Phi^{-1}(\delta\cdot(k+[-1,1]^{d}))=:N_{k}
,
where the right-hand side is a compact subset of
D
. Furthermore,
(\Phi,\kappa)
is a compatible pair and, in particular,
\Phi
is a
(d+1)
-admissible warping function. Hence, by Theorem
2.5
, the space
\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}
is well-defined and we can apply
[
22
, Lemma 2.13]
to obtain a constant
C>0
, such that

(4.10)

\|g_{y,\omega}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\leq Cv(y,\omega),\quad\text{for all}\quad(y,\omega)\in\Lambda.

In particular, for fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\omega\in N_{k}\supset M_{k}
, we obtain
\|g_{y,\omega}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\leq C_{2}(y,k)=:C_{2}
, for some constant
C_{2}(y,k)>0
depending only on
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

It was shown in
[
11
, Lemma 2]
that

\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)
is sequentially weak-
\ast
-dense in

(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
. Hence, there is a sequence
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)\subset(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}

with

\langle f_{n},g\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\langle f,g\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}.

In particular, this means

V_{\theta,\Phi}f_{n}(y,\omega)=\langle f_{n},g_{y,\omega}\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\to\langle f,g_{y,\omega}\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}=V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)

for all
\omega\in M_{k}
.
Furthermore, by the uniform boundedness principle, there is a finite constant
C
, depending on the full sequence
(f_{n})_{n}
, such that
\|f_{n}\|_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}}\leq C<\infty

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and hence

|V_{\theta,\Phi}f_{n}(y,\omega)|=|\langle f_{n},g_{y,\omega}\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}|\leq C\|g_{y,\omega}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}\leq C\cdot C_{2}

for all
\omega\in M_{k}
.
Finally, since
M_{k}\subset N_{k}
with
N_{k}\subset D
compact, we see that
\omega\mapsto[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}

is bounded on
M_{k}
, and that
\mu(M_{k})<\infty
, so that the dominated convergence theorem yields

\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot V_{\theta,\Phi}f_{n}(y,\omega)d\omega\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)d\omega.

As we observed above, the left-hand side of
(
4.8
)
is also
weak-
\ast
-continuous on
(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
, so that (
4.7
)
transfers from
f\in\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)
to arbitrary
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
, as desired.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

Note that, strictly speaking,
[
22
, Lemma 2.13]
only yields (
4.10
) on a dense subset of
\Lambda
. It is, however, easy to see that the estimate extends to all of
\Lambda
if the map
(y,\omega)\mapsto g_{y,\omega}
is (weakly) continuous, which is clearly the case here, see also
[
19
, Proposition 3.4]
.

We are now ready to prove the desired embedding of
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
into
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible. Fix
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, and
0<\vartheta<r-\sqrt{d}/2
. Finally, choose
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\|\theta\|_{1}=1
and
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)\subset\delta\cdot B_{\vartheta}(0)
.

Then, the map
\Psi:(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}\to Z^{\prime}(D)
from Corollary
4.3
restricts
for arbitrary
p,q\in[1,\infty]
to a bounded injective linear map

\Psi\colon\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})

with

u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\qquad\text{ where }\qquad u_{k}:=\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Let
f\in\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\subset(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
be arbitrary.
The assumptions on
\theta
imply that Equation (
4.6
) is satisfied. Hence, Lemma
4.6
and Minkowski’s inequality for integrals
(see e.g.
[
10
, Theorem 6.19]
) yield for arbitrary
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
the estimate

d_{k}:=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\varphi_{k}\cdot\mathcal{F}\psi_{f}]\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}\leq\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}\,d\omega.

Now, let us first consider the case
q<\infty
. In this case, and since we only consider the case
q\geq 1
,
Jensen’s inequality
[
31
, Theorem 3.3]
yields

(4.11)

\begin{split}(u_{k}\cdot d_{k})^{q}&\leq\left[u_{k}\cdot\mu(M_{k})\cdot\int_{M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}\cdot[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\frac{d\omega}{\mu(M_{k})}\right]^{q}\\
&\leq u_{k}^{q}\cdot[\mu(M_{k})]^{q}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\cdot[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-q/2}\frac{d\omega}{\mu(M_{k})},\end{split}

where
\mu
denotes the usual
d
-dimensional Lebesgue measure.

Recall that
v_{0}
is radially increasing and that
w
is
v_{0}^{d}
-moderate, by Theorem
2.5
. Further, note for

\omega\in M_{k}=\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left(k+\smash{\left[\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{d}}\vphantom{\left[\frac{1}{2}\right]}\,\right)\right)

that
\Phi(\omega)\in\delta k+[-\delta,\delta]^{d}\subset\delta k+\overline{B_{d\delta}}(0)
, so that

w(\delta k)=w\left(\Phi(\omega)+[\delta k-\Phi(\omega)]\right)\leq w(\Phi(\omega))\cdot v_{0}^{d}(\delta k-\Phi(\omega))\leq w(\Phi(\omega))\cdot v_{0}^{d}(d\delta\cdot e_{1})=:C_{v_{0},\delta}\cdot w(\Phi(\omega)).

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\mu(M_{k})=\mu\left(\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left(k+[{\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{d}}}\right)\right)\right)=\int_{\delta\cdot\left(k+[{\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{d}}}\right)}|\det(D\Phi^{-1})({\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}})|\,d{\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}}=\int_{\delta\cdot\left(k+[{\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{d}}}\right)}w({\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}})\,d{\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}},

where

w({\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}})=w(\delta k+({\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}}-\delta k))\leq w(\delta k)\cdot v_{0}^{d}({\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}}-\delta k)\leq w(\delta k)\cdot v_{0}^{d}(d\delta\cdot e_{1})\leq C_{v_{0},\delta}\cdot w(\delta k)

for arbitrary
{\lx@scalerel@obj{\Upsilon}}\in\delta\cdot\left[k+[{\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{d}}}\right]\subset\delta k+\overline{B_{d\delta}(0)}
.
Thus,
\mu(M_{k})\leq\delta^{d}\cdot C_{v_{0},\delta}\cdot w(\delta k)
.

Finally, since
\tilde{\kappa}
is radially increasing, we have for
\omega\in M_{k}

\frac{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}(\delta k))}{\kappa(\omega)}=\frac{\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)}{\kappa_{\Phi}(\Phi(\omega))}\leq\tilde{\kappa}(\delta k-\Phi(\omega))\leq\tilde{\kappa}(d\delta\cdot e_{1})=:C_{\tilde{\kappa},\delta}.

Altogether, these considerations show

\displaystyle(u_{k}\cdot d_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq u_{k}^{q}\cdot[\mu(M_{k})]^{q}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\cdot[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-q/2}\frac{d\omega}{\mu(M_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{d(q-1)}\cdot C_{v_{0},\delta}^{3q/2-1}\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{q}{2}-1}\cdot u_{k}^{q}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\,d\omega

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{by def. of }u_{k}}})

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{d(q-1)}\cdot C_{v_{0},\delta}^{3q/2-1}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}[\kappa(\Phi^{-1}(\delta k))]^{q}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\,d\omega

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{with suitable constant }C_{v_{0},\tilde{\kappa},\delta,q}}})

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{0},\kappa,\delta,q}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}[\kappa(\omega)]^{q}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\,d\omega.

Now, since
(\varphi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is a BAPU for
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
by Proposition
3.9
,
we can sum the preceding estimate over
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, to obtain because of
D=\biguplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}M_{k}

(which follows directly from the definition of
M_{k}
and bijectivity of
\Phi
) that

\displaystyle\|\psi_{f}\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}^{q}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(u_{k}\cdot d_{k})^{q}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{0},\kappa,\delta,q}\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{M_{k}}[\kappa(\omega)]^{q}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\,d\omega

\displaystyle=C_{v_{0},\kappa,\delta,q}\cdot\int_{D}\,\,[\kappa(\omega)]^{q}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}^{q}\,d\omega

\displaystyle=C_{v_{0},\kappa,\delta,q}\cdot\|f\|_{\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})}^{q}<\infty,

which completes the proof in case of
q<\infty
.

It remains to consider the case
q=\infty
. Here, we simply note for arbitrary
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
as a consequence of the estimate (
4.11
) (setting
q=1
in that inequality only),

\displaystyle u_{k}\cdot d_{k}

\displaystyle\leq u_{k}\cdot\int_{M_{k}}[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}\,d\omega

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{0},\delta}^{1/2}\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{-1}\cdot\mu(M_{k})\cdot\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\omega\in M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{d}C_{v_{0},\delta}^{3/2}\cdot\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\omega\in M_{k}}\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{0},\kappa,\delta}\cdot\|f\|_{\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,\infty})}<\infty.

Because of
\|\psi_{f}\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{\infty})}=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(u_{k}\cdot d_{k})
, this completes the proof also for
q=\infty
.
∎

4.3.
The embedding

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})

The main technical obstruction to establishing the embedding of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
into
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
is that the action of
f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\subset Z^{\prime}(D)
is only defined on the small space
Z(D)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}
.
Hence, some kind of extension procedure is required to obtain functionals defined on all of
\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}
. We will establish this extension specifically for elements of the decomposition space
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
instead of general elements of
Z^{\prime}(D)
. Nonetheless, our first step is the extension of the voice transform
V_{\theta,\Phi}
to
Z^{\prime}(D)
.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible, and
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. For
f\in Z^{\prime}(D)
, define

V_{\theta,\Phi}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D\to\mathbb{C},(y,\omega)\mapsto\left\langle f,\smash{\overline{\rule{0.0pt}{7.11317pt}g_{y,\omega}}}\,\right\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}.

Remark 4.10
.

By an argument similar to the one in the proof of Corollary
4.3
, we can see that with this definition
V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)=\langle\mathcal{F}f,\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\overline{g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}f](y)
. In particular,
V_{\theta,\Phi}f
is well-defined. Furthermore, by that corollary, the above definition coincides with the definition on
(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}
, for all
f\in(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}(D)
, where the embedding
(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner}\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}(D)
is given by the map
\Psi
defined in Corollary
4.3
.

We proceed to show that the extension of
V_{\theta,\Phi}
to
Z^{\prime}(D)
inherits important properties, such as continuity and the reproducing property. To this end, we will use the following technical lemma.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\varnothing\neq U\subset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
and
\varnothing\neq V\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and let

\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U\times V)
with the following property:

\text{For each compact set $K\subset U$, there is a compact set $L_{K}\subset V$ satisfying }\forall\ u\in K:\,\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Gamma(u,\bullet))\subset L_{K}.

Then the map

\Theta\colon U\to\mathcal{D}(V),u\mapsto\Gamma(u,\bullet)

is well-defined and continuous.

Proof.

By assumption, if we set
K=\{u\}
, for any
u\in U
,
we see that
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Gamma(u,\bullet))\subset L_{\{u\}}\subset V
is compact,
so that
\Gamma(u,\bullet)\in\mathcal{D}(V)
. Hence,
\Theta
is well-defined.

To prove continuity, let
(u_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
U
satisfying
u_{n}\rightarrow u_{0}

for some
u_{0}\in U
. It is not hard to see (using e.g. the definition of compactness) that

K:=\{u_{n}\,:\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\}\subset U
is compact. By assumption, there is thus a compact set
L_{K}\subset V

satisfying
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Gamma(u_{n},\cdot))\subset L_{K}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Now, let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
be arbitrary and let
\varepsilon>0
.
Since
\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\Gamma
is uniformly continuous on the compact set
K\times L_{K}\subset U\times V
,
there is some
\delta>0
satisfying

|(\partial^{\alpha}_{v}\Gamma)(u,v)-(\partial^{\alpha}_{v}\Gamma)(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})|<\varepsilon

for all
u,\tilde{u}\in K
and
v,\tilde{v}\in L_{K}
with
|u-\tilde{u}|<\delta
and
|v-\tilde{v}|<\delta
.
Since
u_{n}\to u_{0}
, there is some
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|u_{n}-u_{0}|<\delta
for all
n\geq n_{0}
.

Now, for
n\geq n_{0}
and
v\in V
, there are two cases:

Case 1
:
v\in L_{K}
. In this case, we have
u_{0},u_{n}\in K
and
v\in L_{K}
with
|u_{n}-u_{0}|<\delta
and

|v-v|=0<\delta
. Hence,

|\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{n})](v)-\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{0})](v)|=|(\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\Gamma)(u_{n},v)-(\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\Gamma)(u_{0},v)|<\varepsilon.

Case 2
:
v\notin L_{K}
. Since
L_{K}
is compact and hence closed, there is some
r>0
with

B_{r}(v)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus L_{K}
. Because of
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Theta(u_{\ell}))\subset L_{K}
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
this implies
\Theta(u_{\ell})\equiv 0
on a neighborhood of
v
, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Hence,

|\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{n})](v)-\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{0})](v)|=|0-0|=0<\varepsilon.

Thus, we have shown that
\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{n})]\to\partial^{\alpha}[\Theta(u_{0})]
uniformly
on
V
for arbitrary
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
, as well as
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Theta(u_{n}))\subset L_{K}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
In view of
[
32
, Theorem 6.5 and the ensuing remark]
, this implies
\Theta(u_{n})\to\Theta(u_{0})
with
convergence in
\mathcal{D}(V)
, as desired. Note that it indeed suffices to consider sequential continuity,
since the domain
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
of
\Theta
is first countable.
∎

Now we are ready to collect the most important properties of the extended voice transform.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible, and
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=1
. Then,

g_{y,\omega}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))
, for all
(y,\omega)\in\Lambda
.
For
f\in Z^{\prime}(D)
, the following hold:

(1)

The voice transform
V_{\theta,\Phi}f
, defined in Definition
4.9
, is continuous.

(2)

For any
h\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D))
, we have
\overline{h}\in Z(D)
and

\langle f,\overline{h}\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}=\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega),\quad\text{for all }f\in Z^{\prime}(D).

(3)

If
K_{\theta,\Phi}\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is given by

K_{\theta,\Phi}((z,\eta),(y,\omega)):=\left\langle g_{y,\omega},\,g_{z,\eta}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}\,g_{z,\eta}(y,\omega)},\quad\text{for all }(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda,

we have

V_{\theta,\Phi}f=K_{\theta,\Phi}(V_{\theta,\Phi}f):=\int_{\Lambda}K_{\theta,\Phi}(\bullet,(y,\omega))\cdot V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)~d(y,\omega),\quad\text{for all }f\in Z^{\prime}(D).

(4)

The map

V_{\theta,\Phi}\colon Z^{\prime}(D)\to\mathcal{C}(\Lambda),f\mapsto V_{\theta,\Phi}f

is injective.

Proof.

To prove Item (1), it suffices by Remark
4.10
to show that the map

\Theta\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(D),\ (y,\omega)\mapsto\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}

is continuous. Note that the map

\Gamma\colon\Lambda\times D\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\ ((y,\omega),\xi)\mapsto\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}(\xi)=[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\overline{\theta(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\omega))}\cdot e^{2\pi i\langle y,\xi\rangle}

is smooth. Here, we used that
\Phi
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism, such that
w=\det(D\Phi^{-1}(\bullet))
is smooth. If
K\subset\Lambda
is compact, then there are
K_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and some compact
K_{1}\subset D
with
K\subset K_{0}\times K_{1}
. This implies, for
(y,\omega)\in K
, that

(4.12)

\begin{split}\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\Gamma((y,\omega),\bullet))&=\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}})=\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g_{\omega})\\
&\subset\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\omega)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))\subset\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(K_{1})+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))=:L_{K_{1}},\end{split}

where
L_{K_{1}}\subset D
is compact. Hence, we can apply Lemma
4.11
to prove continuity of
\Theta
and thus of
V_{\theta,\Phi}f
.

To prove Item (3), note that
\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Hence,
\mathcal{F}g_{y,\omega}=\mathcal{F}(\mathbf{T}_{y}\widecheck{g_{\omega}})=\mathbf{M}_{-y}g_{\omega}\in\mathcal{D}(D)
, for all
(y,\omega)\in\Lambda
, implying
g_{y,\omega}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))
. If we assume for the moment that Item (2) holds, then Item (3) follows by setting
h=g_{z,\eta}
in Item (2).

Furthermore, if Item (2) holds, and
f\in Z^{\prime}(D)
is such that
V_{\theta,\Phi}f\equiv 0
, then

\langle f,h\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}=\left\langle f,\overline{\overline{h}}\right\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}=\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}\overline{h}(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega)=0,\quad\text{for all }h\in Z(D).

Hence
f\equiv 0
, showing that
f\mapsto V_{\theta,\Phi}f
is injective, proving Item (4).

It remains to prove that Item (2) indeed holds. We will instead prove the equivalent identity

(4.13)

\langle F,\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\overline{h})\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D),\mathcal{D}(D)}=\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}F)(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega),

for all
F\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D),\ h\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))
: By elementary properties of the Fourier transform,
h\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))
implies
\overline{h}\in Z(D)
, such that

\langle F,\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D),\mathcal{D}(D)}=\langle\mathcal{F}^{-1}F,\overline{h}\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}
, for all
F\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D),\ h\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))
.

To prove Equation (
4.13
), note that
\mathcal{F}h\in\mathcal{D}(D)
, such that

(4.14)

K:=\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h))-\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))\subset D\quad\text{is compact.}

Now, if
\omega\in D
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

0\neq V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)\overset{(\textrm{Rem.}\ref{rem:ElaborationOfVoiceTransformDef})}{=}\langle\mathcal{F}f,\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}},

then

\varnothing\neq\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h)\cap\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g_{\omega})=\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h)\cap\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\omega)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))=\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h))\cap(\Phi(\omega)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))),

which implies
\Phi(\omega)\in\Phi(\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h))-\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)
and hence
\omega\in K
. Overall, with
L_{K}
as in Equation (
4.12
), we have
L_{K}=\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(K)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))=\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h))-\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta))
. We see that the effective domain of integration in Equation (
4.13
) is
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times K
, and on this set, the support of
\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}
is contained in
L_{K}
.

Now, since
F\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D)
, we can apply
[
32
, Theorem 6.8]
to prove the existence of
C=C(F,h)>0
and
N=N(F,h)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

|\langle F,g\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}}|\leq C\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)},\quad\text{for all }g\in\mathcal{D}(D),\text{ with }\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g)\subset\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h)\cup K\cup L_{K}.

Here,
\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)
is the vector space of all
\mathcal{C}^{N}
-functions
g\colon D\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, such that
\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}:=\max_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},|\alpha|\leq N}\|g\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(D)}<\infty
. The Hahn-Banach theorem then yields a bounded, linear functional
\widetilde{F}\in(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime}
that agrees with
F\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(D)
on all
g\in\mathcal{D}(D)
with
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g)\subset\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h)\cup K\cup L_{K}
. Since the application of bounded linear functionals can be interchanged with Bochner integrals (see
[
24
, Chapter VI, Theorem 4.1]
) and since

V_{\theta,\Phi}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}F)(y,\omega)=\langle F,\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}}=\langle\widetilde{F},\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)},\quad\text{for all }(y,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times K,

we can rewrite the right-hand side of Equation (
4.13
) as

(4.15)

\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}F)(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega)=\left\langle\widetilde{F},\int_{\Lambda}\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}~d(y,\omega)\right\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)},

provided that the (Bochner) integral

(4.16)

\chi_{h}:=\int_{\Lambda}\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}~d(y,\omega)\in\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)

exists. Assume for now that this is the case. Then it only remains to show that
\chi_{h}
coincides with
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}
.
Let
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(D)
be arbitrary and note that
h\mapsto\langle h,\gamma\rangle
is a bounded, linear functional on
\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)
. Since
\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=1
and
h\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{D}(D))\subset\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)
, we obtain by
[
19
, Theorem 3.5]
,

\begin{split}\langle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h},\gamma\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}&=\langle\overline{\mathcal{F}\gamma},h\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\\
&\overset{(\ast)}{=}\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}(\overline{\mathcal{F}\gamma})(y,\omega)\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega)\\
&\overset{(\triangle)}{=}\int_{\Lambda}\langle\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}},\gamma\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega)\\
&\overset{(\square)}{=}\left\langle\int_{\Lambda}\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}~d(y,\omega),\gamma\right\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\\
&=\langle\chi_{h},\gamma\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}.\end{split}

Here, the step marked with
(\ast)
is justified by noting
\overline{\mathcal{F}\gamma}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{\gamma}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\mathcal{C}_{c}(D))\subset\mathbf{L}^{2,\mathcal{F}}(D)
, the step marked with
(\triangle)
by
V_{\theta,\Phi}(\overline{\mathcal{F}\gamma})(y,\omega)=\langle\overline{\mathcal{F}\gamma},\mathbf{T}_{y}\widecheck{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\langle\overline{\gamma},\mathbf{M}_{-y}g_{\omega}\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\langle\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}},\gamma\rangle_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}
. Finally, The step marked with
(\square)
uses the assumption that the integral defining
\chi_{h}
exists and that the application of bounded linear functionals can be interchanged with Bochner integrals (see
[
24
, Chapter VI, Theorem 4.1]
).

Altogether, we have shown that
\chi_{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)\subset\mathcal{C}(D)
and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}=\overline{\mathcal{F}h}\in\mathcal{D}(D)
are two continuous functions that satisfy
\langle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h},\gamma\rangle=\langle\chi_{h},\gamma\rangle
for all
\gamma\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(D)
. Hence,
\chi_{h}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}
. We get

\begin{split}\langle F,\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}\rangle_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}}&=\langle\widetilde{F},\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h}\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}=\langle\widetilde{F},\chi_{h}\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}\\
&=\int_{\Lambda}\langle\widetilde{F},\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\rangle_{(\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D))^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega)\\
&=\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}F)(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega),\end{split}

which is the desired equality (
4.13
).

Finally, we complete the proof by showing that the Bochner integral (
4.16
) does indeed exist. It is well-known that if
X
is a Banach space,
(\Omega,\mu_{\Omega})
a measure space, and
f\colon\Omega\rightarrow X
is Bochner measurable with
\int_{\Omega}\|f\|_{X}~d\mu_{\Omega}<\infty
, then the Bochner integral
\int_{\Omega}f~d\mu_{\Omega}\in X
exists, see, e.g.,
[
24
, Chapter VI, Corollary 5.9]
. We first show Bochner measurability of the integrand in (
4.16
): Recall from the proof of Item (1) that

\Theta\colon D\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(D)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D),\ (y,\omega)\mapsto\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}
is continuous. Since
V_{\theta,\Phi}h\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is continuous as well, see Section
2.1
, the integrand in (
4.16
) is continuous as a map into
\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)
. Since the domain
\Lambda=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D\subset\mathbb{R}^{2d}
is separable, this implies that the integrand is separable-valued and Borel measurable (by continuity). By
[
24
, Chapter VI, M11 (Page 124)]
, this implies that the integrand is Bochner measurable.

Now, we proceed to show that
\int_{\Lambda}\|\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}~d(y,\omega)<\infty
. Since
\Theta
is continuous, see above, the map
D\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(D),\ \omega\mapsto\overline{g_{\omega}}=\Theta(0,\omega)
is continuous as well. Since
\|\bullet\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}
is a continuous semi-norm on
\mathcal{D}(D)
, we conclude that
\max_{\omega\in K}\|\overline{g_{\omega}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}
is finite. Therefore, Leibniz’ rule yields, for
(y,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times K
,
\xi\in D
, and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
with
|\alpha|\leq N
, that

|\partial^{\alpha}[\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}](\xi)|\leq\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}|\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}e^{2\pi i\langle y,\xi\rangle}|\cdot|(\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\overline{g_{\omega}})(\xi)|\lesssim\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}|2\pi y|^{\beta}\lesssim(1+|y|)^{N},

where the final implied constant depends on
N
,
K
,
\Phi
and
\theta
. In short,
\|\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}\lesssim(1+|y|)^{N}
, for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\omega\in K
.

Since
\overline{h}\in Z(D)
with
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\overline{h})=\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\mathcal{F}h)=:\tilde{K}
compact, Lemma
4.2
yields
C_{2}=C_{2}(d,N,\theta,\tilde{K},\Phi)>0
, such that

|V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)|\leq C_{2}\cdot(1+|y|)^{-(d+N+1)}{\mathds{1}}_{K}(\omega),\quad\text{for all }(y,\omega)\in\Lambda.

Here, we implicitly used that
V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)=0
whenever
\omega\in D\setminus K
, with
K
as in Equation (
4.14
), which we already noted in the discussion after said equation. Overall, this implies

\int_{\Lambda}\|\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{y}\overline{g_{\omega}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{N}_{b}(D)}~d(y,\omega)\lesssim\cdot\int_{\Lambda}(1+|y|)^{N}\cdot(1+|y|)^{-(d+N+1)}\cdot{\mathds{1}}_{K}(\omega)~d(y,\omega)<\infty,

since
K\subset D
is compact. The final implied constant here has the same dependencies as
C_{2}
above. We conclude that the Bochner integral (
4.16
) exists and the proof is finished.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

In Lemma
4.12
, we assume that
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is such that
\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=1
. If that is not the case, it suffices to note that
V_{\theta,\Phi}f
is conjugate linear in the choice of
\theta
to see that Lemma
4.12
remains valid, up to modification by a constant factor in Items
2
and
3
. Specifically, we have

\langle f,\overline{h}\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}=\|\theta\|^{-2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta,\Phi}f(y,\omega)\cdot\overline{V_{\theta,\Phi}h(y,\omega)}~d(y,\omega),

and

V_{\theta,\Phi}f=\|\theta\|^{-2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot K_{\theta,\Phi}(V_{\theta,\Phi}f),

for all
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
f\in Z^{\prime}(D)
.

The next result establishes the main part of the embedding

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
,
by showing
\|V_{\theta,\Phi}f\|_{\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible and fix
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, and
0<t<r-\sqrt{d}/2
. Finally, choose
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that Equation (
4.6
) is satisfied.

For
p,q\in[1,\infty]
and

u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\qquad\text{ with }u_{k}:=\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}},

there is a constant
C>0
such that each

f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
satisfies

\|V_{\theta,\Phi}f\|_{\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}}\leq C\cdot\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}<\infty.

Proof.

Let
f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\subset Z^{\prime}(D)
be fixed.
As seen in Remark
4.10
, we have

(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)=[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\overline{\rule{0.0pt}{7.11317pt}g_{\omega}}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)](y)=[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(g_{\omega}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)](y)\quad\text{for all}\quad(y,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D,

since
\theta
, and thus also
g_{\omega}
, is real. Assume for now that

\omega\in M_{k}:=\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left(k+\smash{\left[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right)^{d}}\vphantom{\left[\frac{1}{2}\right]}\,\right)\right)
.
Since
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)\subset\delta\cdot B_{t}(0)
, with
0<t<\sqrt{r}/2
, this implies, by (
3.6
),

\displaystyle\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g_{\omega})=\Phi^{-1}\left(\Phi(\omega)+\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\subset\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left[k+\smash{\left[\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{d}}\vphantom{\left[\textstyle{\frac{1}{2}}\right]}\,\right]+\delta\cdot B_{t}(0)\right)

\displaystyle\subset\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta k+\delta\cdot B_{\sqrt{d}/2}(0)+\delta\cdot B_{t}(0)\right)

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{since }t+\sqrt{d}/2<r}})

\displaystyle\subset\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta[k+B_{r}(0)]\right)=Q_{\Phi,k}^{(\delta,r)}.

But since
(\varphi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-BAPU, we have for

\varphi_{k}^{\ast}:=\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}\varphi_{\ell}
, with
k^{\ast}=\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}~:~Q_{\Phi,\ell}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi,k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\}
, that

\varphi_{k}^{\ast}\equiv 1
on
Q_{k}^{(\delta,r)}\supset\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g_{\omega})
.
Hence,
g_{\omega}=\varphi_{k}^{\ast}\cdot g_{\omega}
and thus

(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)=[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(g_{\omega}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)](y)=[\mathcal{F}^{-1}(g_{\omega}\cdot\varphi_{k}^{\ast}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)](y)\quad\text{for all}\quad\omega\in M_{k}.

Using Young’s inequality and Lemma
3.8

(and recalling
g_{\omega}=\sqrt{w(\Phi(\omega))}\cdot\varphi^{(\omega)}
), we thus obtain for all
\omega\in M_{k}
:

\displaystyle\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}g_{\omega}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1}}\cdot\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{k}^{\ast}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{Lem. }\ref{lem:BAPUBuildingBlocksEstimate}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}

\displaystyle=[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell},\quad\text{ with }d_{\ell}:=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{\ell}\cdot\mathcal{F}f)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}},\quad\text{for all}\quad\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

Here, the implied constant is provided by Lemma
3.8
, with
\zeta=\theta
.

Note that
M_{k}\subset Q_{\Phi,k}^{(\delta,r)}
, since
r>\sqrt{d}/2
and define
v^{\prime}:=\kappa_{\Phi}\cdot w^{-1/2}
. The weight
v^{\prime}
is
v
-moderate with
v:=\tilde{\kappa}\cdot v_{0}^{d/2}
, where
v_{0}
is a control weight for
\Phi
, see also

[
19
, Lemma 4.9]
. Thus, an application of Lemma
3.2
, Item (1), with
\tilde{v}=v^{\prime}\circ\Phi=\kappa\cdot(w\circ\Phi)^{-1/2}
shows for all
\omega\in M_{k}\subset Q^{(\delta,r)}_{k}
,

(4.17)

\begin{split}F(\omega)&:=\kappa(\omega)\cdot\|(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(\cdot,\omega)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{p}}\\
&\lesssim\kappa(\omega)[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1/2}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell}\\
({\scriptstyle|\delta k-\Phi(\omega)|<\delta r})&\lesssim\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{-1/2}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell}\\
&=\frac{u_{k}}{[w(\delta k)]^{1/q}}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell}.\end{split}

Note that since the weight
u
is
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-moderate
(cf. Lemma
4.5
), the so-called
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
-clustering map

\Gamma\colon\ell_{u}^{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\to\ell_{u}^{q}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\quad(c_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\mapsto(c_{k}^{\ast})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}},\quad\text{with}\quad c_{k}^{\ast}:=\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}c_{\ell}

is well-defined and bounded; see
e.g.
[
7
, Definition 2.5 and Lemma 3.2]
.
This implies
(d_{k}^{\ast})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\in\ell_{u}^{q}
with

\|(d_{k}^{\ast})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|_{\ell_{u}^{q}}\leq\|\Gamma\|\cdot\|(d_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|_{\ell_{u}^{q}}=\|\Gamma\|\cdot\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}
.

Now, we distinguish the two cases
q<\infty
and
q=\infty
.
For
q<\infty
, we take the
q
-th power of (
4.17
) and integrate over

\omega\in M_{k}
to obtain

\int_{M_{k}}[F(\omega)]^{q}\,d\omega\lesssim\frac{\mu(M_{k})}{w(\delta k)}\cdot\left(u_{k}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell}\right)^{q}.

Next, note that since
w
is
v_{0}^{d}
-moderate and
v_{0}
radially increasing, we have (with
\Phi(M_{k})=\delta k+[-\delta/2,\delta/2]
)

\begin{split}\mu(M_{k})&=\mu\left(\Phi^{-1}\left(\delta\cdot\left[k+\smash{\left[\textstyle{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{d}}\vphantom{\left[\textstyle{\frac{1}{2}}\right]}\,\right]\right)\right)\\
&=\int_{\Phi(M_{k})}w(\tau)\,d\tau\leq\int_{\Phi(M_{k})}w(\delta k)\cdot v_{0}^{d}(\tau-\delta k)\,d\tau\\
&\leq\delta^{d}\cdot v_{0}^{d}(\delta\sqrt{d})\cdot w(\delta k).\end{split}

Thus, with an appropriately large constant
C>0
, depending on
\Phi
,
\theta
and the control weight
v_{0}
, as well as
d
and
\delta
, we have
\int_{M_{k}}[F(\omega)]^{q}\,d\omega\leq C\cdot(u_{k}\cdot c_{k})^{q}
for all

k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. But since
D=\biguplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}M_{k}
, we can sum this estimate over
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to conclude

\displaystyle\|V_{\theta,\Phi}f\|_{\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}}^{q}

\displaystyle=\int_{D}[F(\omega)]^{q}\,d\omega=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{M_{k}}[F(\omega)]^{q}\,d\omega

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(u_{k}\cdot c_{k})^{q}=C\cdot\|c\|_{\ell_{u}^{q}}^{q}

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\|\Gamma\|^{q}\cdot\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}^{q}<\infty.

This proves the claim in case of
q<\infty
.

For
q=\infty
, the proof is slightly simpler: From Equation (
4.17
), and denoting by
\tilde{C}>0
the implied constant there, we get

\displaystyle F(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\cdot u_{k}\cdot\sum_{\ell\in k^{\ast}}d_{\ell}=\tilde{C}\cdot u_{k}\cdot d_{k}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\cdot\|(d_{k}^{\ast})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|_{\ell_{u}^{\infty}}\leq\tilde{C}\cdot\|\Gamma\|\cdot\|d\|_{\ell_{u}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\cdot\|\Gamma\|\cdot\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})}<\infty,

which proves the claim, since
q=\infty
implies

\|V_{\theta,\Phi}f\|_{\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}}=\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\omega\in D}F(\omega)
.
∎

Now, we can finally show our claim that the coorbit space
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
is canonically isomorphic to
the decomposition space
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})
.

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible, see Definition
2.4
, and fix
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, and
0<t<r-\sqrt{d}/2
. Finally, choose
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that Equation (
4.6
) is satisfied.

For
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, the bounded linear map

\Psi\colon\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q}),\quad f\mapsto\psi_{f}\quad\text{with}\quad\psi_{f}:Z(D)\to\mathbb{C},\quad g\mapsto f(\overline{g})=\langle f,\overline{g}\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}.

is an isomorphism of Banach spaces. Here,
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
,
u_{k}:=\kappa_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, as before.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.8
and the bounded inverse theorem, it suffices to show that
\Psi
is surjective.
Thus, let
f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\subset Z^{\prime}(D)
be arbitrary,
and let
F:=V_{\theta,\Phi}f
.
By Lemma
4.14
, we have
F\in\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}
.
Furthermore, Lemma
4.12
and Remark
4.13
show that
F=\|\theta\|^{-2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot K_{\theta,\Phi}(F)
, where

K_{\theta,\Phi}
is as in the lemma.

Apply
[
22
, Lemma 2.30]
(with the reproducing kernel
R=\|\theta\|^{-2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot K_{\theta,\Phi}
) to see that
F=V_{\theta,\Phi}f_{0}
for some
f_{0}\in\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
.
With
f:=\Psi f_{0}=\psi_{f_{0}}
, Theorem
4.8
shows

f\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\subset Z^{\prime}(D)
.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)

\displaystyle=\left\langle f,\overline{g_{y,\omega}}\right\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\psi_{f_{0}},\overline{g_{y,\omega}}\right\rangle_{Z^{\prime}(D),Z(D)}

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{Def. of }\psi_{f_{0}}}})

\displaystyle=\left\langle f_{0},g_{y,\omega}\right\rangle_{(\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1})^{\urcorner},\mathcal{H}_{v}^{1}}

\displaystyle=(V_{\theta,\Phi}f_{0})(y,\omega)=G(y,\omega)=(V_{\theta,\Phi}f)(y,\omega)

for all
(y,\omega)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D
.

But as shown in Lemma
4.12
,
this implies
f=\Psi f_{0}\in\mathrm{image}(\Psi)
, so that
\Psi
is indeed surjective and the proof is complete.
∎

At last, we drop our special assumptions regarding
\theta
.

Theorem 4.16
.

Let
\Phi:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible, see Definition
2.4
,
and let
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Further, fix
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
and consider the
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
induced by
\Phi
, see Definition
1.1
.

The map
\Psi
defined in Corollary
4.3
restricts to an isomorphism of Banach spaces

\Psi:\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})\quad\text{ with }\quad u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\text{ and }u_{k}=\kappa(\Phi^{-1}(\delta k))\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\,\,.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.5
, the coorbit space

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})=\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})

is independent of the choice of
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Hence, the result follows immediately from
Corollary
4.15
.
∎

5.
Fundamentals of radial warping

The frequency coverings associated with well-known instances of decomposition spaces, say Besov or
\alpha
-modulation spaces, exhibit symmetries with respect to the modulus of the frequency position. The same can be said about related constructions of frame decompositions. This observation has motivated the study of
radial warping functions
in
[
19
]
. Since such radially warped time-frequency systems feature prominently in our embedding results presented in Sections
6
–
9
. Finally, we obtain conditions for equality of warped coorbit spaces and embeddings between warped coorbit spaces with respect to radial warping functions, in Section , we shortly recall the most important results on radial warping presented in
[
19
]
.

Definition 5.1

(
[
19
, Definition 8.3]
)
.

For a diffeomorphism
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, with
\varrho(\xi)=c\xi
for all

\xi\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
and some
\varepsilon,c>0
, the

associated radial warping function
is given by

(5.1)

\Phi_{\varrho}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\xi\mapsto\widetilde{\varrho}(|\xi|)\cdot\xi,\quad\text{with}\quad\widetilde{\varrho}(t):=\varrho(t)/t\quad\text{for }t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\,,\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\varrho}(0):=c\,.

For a more concise notation and to stay consistent with
[
19
]
we denote by
\varrho_{\ast}:=\varrho^{-1}
the inverse of a bijection
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
. If
\varrho
satisfies some more structured assumptions, specified in the following definition, then
\Phi_{\varrho}
is a
k
-admissible warping function in the sense of Definition
2.2
, see below.

Definition 5.2

(
[
19
, Definition 8.1]
)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. A function
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is called a

k
-admissible radial component with control weight

v:\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty)
,
if the following hold:

(1)

\varrho
is a strictly increasing
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
-diffeomorphism with inverse

\varrho_{\ast}=\varrho^{-1}
.

(2)

\varrho
is antisymmetric, that is,
\varrho(-\xi)=-\varrho(\xi)
for all

\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular,
\varrho(0)=0
.

(3)

There are
\varepsilon>0
and
c>0
with
\varrho(\xi)=c\xi

for all
\xi\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
.

(4)

The weight
v
is continuous, submultiplicative,
and radially increasing. Additionally,
\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}
and

(5.2)

\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}:\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty),\qquad\text{defined by}\qquad\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\xi):=\varrho_{\ast}(\xi)/\xi\quad\text{for}\quad\xi\neq 0,\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(0):=c^{-1}.

are
v
-moderate.

(5)

There are constants
C_{0},C_{1}>0
with

(5.3)

C_{0}\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\xi)\leq\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)\leq C_{1}\cdot(1+\xi)\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\xi)\qquad\forall\,\xi\in(0,\infty).

(6)

We have
|\varrho_{\ast}^{(\ell)}|\lesssim\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}
, for all
\ell\in\underline{k+1}
.

Remark 5.3
.

Note that here, we use a simpler, equivalent form of Item (6) already mentioned in
[
19
]
, after Definition 8.1 therein. An analogous simplification applies in the case of weakly admissible radial components, see Definition
5.5
below.

Theorem 5.4

(
[
19
, Proposition 8.7, Corollary 8.8]
)
.

Let
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
k
-admissible radial component, for some

k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\geq d+1
, with control weight
v:\mathbb{R}\!\to\!(0,\infty)
.
Then the associated radial warping function

\Phi_{\varrho}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a
k
-admissible warping function. Furthermore, there is a constant
C>0
, such that the control weight
v_{0}
for
\Phi_{\varrho}
can be chosen as

(5.4)

v_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to(0,\infty),\tau\mapsto C\cdot(1+|\tau|)\cdot v(|\tau|),

and the weight
w=\det(\mathrm{D}\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1})
is given by

(5.5)

w(\tau)=\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\tau|)\cdot[\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(|\tau|)]^{d-1}\,.

The technical requirement that
\varrho
be linear in a neighborhood of the origin (Condition (3) in Definition
5.2
) is often unnatural, but for every function
\varsigma
that satisfies the following weaker assumption, an
equivalent

k
-admissible radial component
\varrho
can be constructed, see also Remark
6.7
for a discussion of said equivalence.

Definition 5.5

(
[
19
, Definition 8.12]
)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
A continuous function
\varsigma:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

is called a
weakly
k
-admissible radial component with control weight

u:[0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
, if it satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

\varsigma
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
on
(0,\infty)
, with
\varsigma^{\prime}(\xi)>0

for all
\xi\in(0,\infty)
. In particular,
\varsigma_{\ast}=\varsigma^{-1}
exists and is
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
on its domain.

(2)

\varsigma(0)=0
and
\varsigma(\xi)\to\infty
as
\xi\to\infty
.

(3)

The control weight
u
is continuous and increasing with

u(\xi+\eta)\leq u(\xi)\cdot u(\eta)
for all
\xi,\eta\in[0,\infty)
.
Furthermore, there are
\delta>0
and
C_{0},C_{1}>0
, such that the following properties hold for all
\xi,\eta\notin B_{\delta}(0)
:

(5.6)

\displaystyle C_{0}\cdot\widetilde{\varsigma_{\ast}}(\xi)\leq\varsigma_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)\leq C_{1}\cdot\varsigma_{\ast}(\xi),

(5.7)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varsigma_{\ast}}(\xi)\leq\widetilde{\varsigma_{\ast}}(\eta)\cdot u(|\xi-\eta|),

(5.8)

\displaystyle|\varsigma_{\ast}^{(\ell)}(\xi)|\lesssim\varsigma_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)

\displaystyle\qquad\forall\,\ell\in\underline{k+1}\,,

where
\widetilde{\varsigma_{\ast}}(\eta)=\varsigma_{\ast}(\eta)/\eta
, for all
\eta\in(0,\infty)
, analogous to
\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}
in (
5.2
).

Theorem 5.6

(
[
19
, Proposition 8.14]
)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and let
\varsigma:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be a
weakly
k
-admissible radial component with control weight

u:[0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
.

Fix some
\varepsilon>0
, some

c\in\big(0,\varsigma(\varepsilon)/(2\varepsilon)\big)
,
and an even function
\Omega\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
that satisfies

\Omega(\xi)=1
for
x\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)
,
\Omega(\xi)=0
for
x\not\in B_{2\varepsilon}(0)
,
and
\Omega^{\prime}(\xi)\leq 0
for
\xi\in[0,\infty)
.
Then the function

(5.9)

\varrho\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},\ \xi\mapsto\begin{cases}c\xi\cdot\Omega(\xi)+\mathop{\operatorname{sgn}}(\xi)\cdot(1-\Omega(\xi))\cdot\varsigma(|\xi|),&\text{if }\xi\neq 0\,,\\
0,&\text{if }\xi=0\,,\end{cases}

is a
k
-admissible radial component. There is a a constant
C\geq 1
, such that

v\colon\mathbb{R}\to(0,\infty),\ \xi\mapsto C\cdot u(|\xi|)\,

can be chosen as control weight for
\varrho
.

Remark 5.7
.

In
[
19
]
, the radial component
\varrho
constructed in Theorem
5.6
is referred to as a
slow start version
of
\varsigma
. We will adopt this terminology in the following sections. Although
\Phi_{\varsigma}:\xi\mapsto\xi/|\xi|\cdot\varsigma(|\xi|)
is not a warping function in the sense of Definition
2.1
, it is continuous and invertible, such that we can define the induced
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency coverings
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varsigma}}^{(\delta,r)}:=\big[\,Q_{\Phi_{\varsigma},k}^{(\delta,r)}\,\big]_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
by
Q_{\Phi_{\varsigma},k}^{(\delta,r)}:=\Phi_{\varsigma}^{-1}(\delta\cdot B_{r}(k))
, for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, as in Definition
1.1
. The coverings
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varsigma}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}
coincide up to finitely many covering elements which have nontrivial intersection with the ball
B_{2\varepsilon}(0)
. It is thus easy to show that they are weakly equivalent. Since all the elements of either covering are path-connected, they are equivalent by Lemma
2.14
and, by Theorem
2.13
, generate the same decomposition spaces (in the sense of equivalent norms). Strictly speaking, it must be verified that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varsigma}}
is indeed a decomposition covering. This, however, is a straightforward task, which only requires the modification of the BAPU in Definition
3.7
for the finite number of elements in
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varsigma}}
that differ from those in
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}
.

6.
Embedding relation between warped coorbit spaces

We begin by recalling some results from
[
36
]
, which will be useful for the verification of embedding relations between pairs of warped coorbit spaces in this section and between Besov spaces and warped coorbit spaces in Section
7
. In Sections
8
and
9
we will further show that
\alpha
-modulation spaces and certain spaces of dominating mixed smoothness coincide with certain warped coorbit spaces. This will be accomplished by verifying the conditions of Theorem
2.13
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\varnothing\neq D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and let
\mathcal{Q}=(Q_{i})_{i\in I}=(T_{i}Q^{\prime}_{i}+b_{i})_{i\in I}
and
\mathcal{P}=(P_{j})_{j\in J}=(S_{j}P^{\prime}_{j}+c_{j})_{j\in J}
be two open, tight, semi-structured coverings of
D
, and
v=(v_{j})_{j\in J}
a
\mathcal{P}
-moderate weight. We say that
v
is
relatively
\mathcal{Q}
-moderate
, if

\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{k,\ell\in\{j\in J\,:\,P_{j}\cap Q_{i}\neq\varnothing\}}v_{k}/v_{\ell}<\infty,

and
\mathcal{P}
is
relatively
\mathcal{Q}
-moderate
, if

\sup_{i\in I}\sup_{k,\ell\in\{j\in J\,:\,P_{j}\cap Q_{i}\neq\varnothing\}}|\det(S_{k})|/|\det(S_{\ell})|<\infty.

In the following theorem, we use the convention that the conjugate exponent
p^{\prime}
is defined for
p\in(0,1]
to equal
p^{\prime}=\infty
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
\varnothing\neq D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and let
\mathcal{Q}=(Q_{i})_{i\in I}=(T_{i}Q^{\prime}_{i}+b_{i})_{i\in I}
and
\mathcal{P}=(P_{j})_{j\in J}=(S_{j}P^{\prime}_{j}+c_{j})_{j\in J}
be two open, tight, semi-structured coverings of
D
. Furthermore, let
u=(u_{i})_{i\in I}
be
\mathcal{Q}
-moderate and
v=(v_{j})_{j\in J}
be
\mathcal{P}
-moderate, and let
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
. Assume that
\mathcal{P}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}
and choose for each
i\in I
some
j_{i}\in J
with
Q_{i}\cap P_{j_{i}}\neq\varnothing
. If
\mathcal{P}
and
v
are relatively
\mathcal{Q}
-moderate. Then the following hold:

(1)

[
36
, Theorem 7.2 (4)]

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell^{q_{1}}_{u})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{P},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2}},\ell^{q_{2}}_{v})
if and only if

p_{1}\leq p_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{t}:=\left\|\left(\frac{v_{j_{i}}}{u_{i}}\cdot|\det T_{i}|^{t}\cdot|\det S_{j_{i}}|^{p_{1}^{-1}-p_{2}^{-1}-t}\right)_{i\in I}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}}<\infty,

with
t=\max(0,q_{2}^{-1}-\min(p_{1}^{-1},1-p_{1}^{-1}))
.

(2)

[
36
, Theorem 7.4 (4)]

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{P},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell^{q_{1}}_{v})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2}},\ell^{q_{2}}_{u})
if and only if

p_{1}\leq p_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad K_{\tilde{t}}:=\left\|\left(\frac{u_{i}}{v_{j_{i}}}\cdot|\det T_{i}|^{\tilde{t}}\cdot|\det S_{j_{i}}|^{p_{1}^{-1}-p_{2}^{-1}-\tilde{t}}\right)_{i\in I}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}}<\infty,

with
\tilde{t}=\max(0,\max(p_{2}^{-1},1-p_{2}^{-1})-q_{1}^{-1})
.

(3)

[
36
, Corollary 7.3]
If
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}
, then
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell^{q_{1}}_{u})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2}},\ell^{q_{2}}_{v})
if and only if

p_{1}\leq p_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad K:=\left\|\left(\frac{v_{i}}{u_{i}}\cdot|\det T_{i}|^{p_{1}^{-1}-p_{2}^{-1}}\right)_{i\in I}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}}<\infty.

Interpreting warped coorbit spaces as decomposition spaces provides access to Theorem
6.2
for proving embedding relations between spaces
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{1},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1},q_{1}}_{\kappa_{1}})
and
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{2},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{\kappa_{2}})
. In general, for an embedding
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{1},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1},q_{1}}_{\kappa_{1}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{2},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{\kappa_{2}})
, Theorem
6.2
suggests that
p_{1}\leq p_{2}
is necessary, such that we can assume
p_{\delta}=p_{1}^{-1}-p_{2}^{-1}\geq 0
.

6.1.
Embeddings between warped coorbit spaces generated by the same warping function

For now, assume that
\Phi=\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{2}
and
(\Phi,\kappa_{1})
and
(\Phi,\kappa_{2})
form compatible pairs as per Definition
2.4
. In particular,
(\kappa_{j})_{\Phi}=\kappa_{j}\circ\Phi^{-1}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
, is
\tilde{\kappa_{j}}
-moderate, where
\tilde{\kappa_{j}}
is continuous, submultiplicative and radially increasing. Recall from Lemma
3.2
and
Proposition
3.9
that the induced
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
, with
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
is tight and semi-structured. Under these assumptions, Theorem
4.16
yields

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{1},q_{1}}_{\kappa_{1}})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell_{u^{(q_{1})}}^{q_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{\kappa_{2}})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2}},\ell_{v^{(q_{2})}}^{q_{2}})

up to canonical identifications, with the sequence elements of
u^{(q_{1})},v^{(q_{2})}
given by

u^{(q_{1})}_{k}=(\kappa_{1})_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad v^{(q_{2})}_{k}=(\kappa_{2})_{\Phi}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

Finally, the linear maps
T_{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, in Definition
2.7
(2) take the form
T_{k}=A(\delta k)=D\Phi^{-1}(\delta k)
, such that
|\det T_{k}|=w(\delta k)
, see Lemma
3.2
.

Overall, we can apply Theorem
6.2
(3) to show that the relation
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{1},q_{1}}_{\kappa_{1}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{\kappa_{2}})

holds if and only if

(6.1)

p_{1}\leq p_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad K:=\left\|\Big([w(\delta k)]^{p_{\delta}-q_{\delta}}\cdot\frac{(\kappa_{2})_{\Phi}(\delta k)}{(\kappa_{1})_{\Phi}(\delta k)}\Big)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}}<\infty\quad\text{with }q_{\delta}=q_{1}^{-1}-q_{2}^{-2}.

If
q_{1}\leq q_{2}
, then
(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}=\infty
such that the above simplifies to
(\kappa_{2})_{\Phi}\lesssim w^{q_{\delta}-p_{\delta}}\cdot(\kappa_{1})_{\Phi}
, which is equivalent to

\kappa_{2}\lesssim[w\circ\Phi]^{q_{\delta}-p_{\delta}}\cdot\kappa_{1}.

If even
\kappa_{1}\asymp\kappa_{2}
, we simply obtain
[w(\delta k)]^{p_{\delta}-q_{\delta}}\lesssim 1
. If on the other hand
q_{1}>q_{2}
, then (
6.1
) depends heavily on the integrability of
w^{p_{\delta}-q_{\delta}}
or the compensation of said term’s non-integrability by means of a weight
\kappa_{1}
with sufficient growth.
For example, if
\kappa_{2}\equiv 1
, then it is easy to verify that

\begin{split}\text{\eqref{eq:SimplifiedWarpedEmbeddingCond} fails if}\quad\kappa_{1}&\lesssim[w\circ\Phi]^{p_{\delta}-q_{\delta}}\cdot[1+|\Phi(\bullet)|]^{\frac{d}{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}},\\
\text{but \eqref{eq:SimplifiedWarpedEmbeddingCond} holds if}\quad\kappa_{1}&\gtrsim[w\circ\Phi]^{p_{\delta}-q_{\delta}}\cdot[1+|\Phi(\bullet)|]^{\frac{d+\varepsilon}{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}},\text{ for some }\varepsilon>0.\end{split}

6.2.
Embeddings between warped coorbit spaces generated by different warping functions

Here, we only consider the case
p=p_{1}=p_{2}
and
q=q_{1}=q_{2}
. Specifically, Theorem
6.4
introduces a condition that yields equality of warped coorbit spaces, whereas Theorem
6.9
studies embeddings between warped coorbit spaces in the important special case of radial warping functions. As preparation, the next lemma is concerned with subordinateness relations between the
(\delta,r)
-coverings induced by
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
be two
0
-admissible warping functions. If there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

(6.2)

\|D\Phi_{2}(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\tau))\cdot D\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\tau)\|\leq C,\quad\text{for all }\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

then
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}_{\Phi_{1}}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}_{\Phi_{2}}
, for all
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
and
r_{1},r_{2}>\sqrt{d}/2
.

Given a weight
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
\kappa\circ\Phi_{2}^{-1}
is
\tilde{\kappa}
-moderate for some continuous, submultiplicative, and radially increasing weight
\tilde{\kappa}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
, we additionally have

(6.3)

\kappa(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\delta_{1}k))\asymp\kappa(\Phi_{2}^{-1}(\delta_{2}\ell)),\quad\text{for all }k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\text{ with }Q_{\Phi_{1},k}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}\cap Q_{\Phi_{2},\ell}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}\neq\varnothing.

In particular, if
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}
are
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-diffeomorphisms and
(d+1)
-admissible warping functions, then compatibility of
\Phi_{2}
and
\kappa
in the sense of Definition
2.4
implies that
\Phi_{1}
and
\kappa
are compatible as well.

Proof.

Our goal is to show that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}_{\Phi_{1}}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}_{\Phi_{2}}
, for all
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
and
r_{1},r_{2}>\sqrt{d}/2
. To this end, we begin by showing that, for a given
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/{2}
, and provided that (
6.2
) holds, there exists
R>\sqrt{d}/2
, such that
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{1}}^{(\delta,r)}
is subordinate, and thereby almost subordinate, to
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{2}}^{(\delta,R)}
. Since almost subordinateness is a transitive relation, and equivalent covers are almost subordinate to one another, Lemma
3.2
, which showed the equivalence of all
\Phi
-induced
(\delta,r)
-fine coverings, can be applied to
\Phi_{1}
and to
\Phi_{2}
in order to obtain the result for arbitrary
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
and
r_{1},r_{2}>\sqrt{d}/2
.

Fix any
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
. Since
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{2}}^{(\delta,r)}
is a covering of
D
by Lemma
3.2
, there exists, for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, a
j=j(k)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, such that
\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\delta k)\in\Phi^{-1}_{2}(\delta\cdot B_{r}(j))
, or equivalently

(6.4)

\Phi_{2}(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\delta k))\in B_{\delta r}(\delta j).

By the chain rule, (
6.2
) states that

\|D(\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1})(\tau)\|=\|D\Phi_{2}(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\tau))\cdot D\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\tau)\|\leq C,

for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e.,
\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
is Lipschitz-continuous with Lipschitz constant no larger than
C
. If
\xi\in Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}
, then
\tau:=\Phi_{1}(\xi)\in B_{\delta r}(\delta k)
, and thus

\left|\Phi_{2}(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\tau))-\Phi_{2}(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\delta k))\right|<C\delta r.

Together with (
6.4
) and
R:=r+C\delta r/\delta=(C+1)r
, we obtain
\Phi_{2}(\xi)=\Phi_{2}(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\tau))\in\delta B_{R}(j)
and thus
\tau\in Q^{(\delta,R)}_{\Phi_{2},j}
. Since the choice of
\xi\in Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}
was arbitrary, this implies

Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}\subset Q^{(\delta,R)}_{\Phi_{2},j}.

Since further
R=(C+1)r
is independent of the choice of
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{1}}^{(\delta,r)}
is subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{2}}^{(\delta,R)}
, proving the first part of the lemma, as discussed in the first paragraph of the proof.

To prove the second part, assume that
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
are such that
Q_{\Phi_{1},k}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}\cap Q_{\Phi_{2},\ell}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}\neq\varnothing
and let
\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{1},k}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}\cap Q_{\Phi_{2},\ell}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}
. This implies

|\Phi_{1}(\xi)-\delta_{1}k|<\delta_{1}r_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad|\Phi_{2}(\xi)-\delta_{2}\ell|<\delta_{2}r_{2}.

Using the Lipschitz-continuity of
\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}
, see above, and the triangle inequality, we conclude that

|\Phi_{2}(\xi)-\Phi_{2}(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\delta_{1}k))|<C\delta_{1}r_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad|\delta_{2}\ell-\Phi_{2}(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\delta_{1}k))|<\delta_{2}r_{2}+C\delta_{1}r_{1}.

With this estimate and the
\tilde{\kappa}
-moderateness of
\kappa\circ\Phi_{2}
, it follows that

\frac{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\delta_{1}k))}{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{2}(\delta_{2}\ell))}=\frac{(\kappa\circ\Phi^{-1}_{2})((\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1})(\delta_{1}k))}{(\kappa\circ\Phi^{-1}_{2})(\delta_{2}\ell)}\leq\tilde{\kappa}((\delta_{2}r_{2}+C\delta_{1}r_{1})\cdot e_{1}),

and the lower bound
\tfrac{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\delta_{1}k))}{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{2}(\delta_{2}k))}\geq[\tilde{\kappa}((\delta_{2}r_{2}+C\delta_{1}r_{1})\cdot e_{1}]^{-1}
is obtained analogously. Here, we used that
\tilde{\kappa}
is radially increasing.

The statement about compatibility of
\Phi_{1},\ \Phi_{2}
and
\kappa
now follows by noting that the assumptions on
\kappa
are exactly those in Definition
2.4
, whereas the calculation

\frac{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\tau))}{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\sigma))}=\frac{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{2}(\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\tau)))}{\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{2}(\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\sigma)))}\leq\tilde{\kappa}(|\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\tau)-\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1}(\sigma)|\cdot e_{1})\leq\tilde{\kappa}(C\cdot|\tau-\sigma|\cdot e_{1})=\tilde{\kappa}(C\cdot(\tau-\sigma))

shows that
\kappa\circ\Phi^{-1}_{1}
is
(\tilde{\kappa}(C\bullet))
-moderate, i.e.,
\Phi_{1}
and
\kappa
satisfy the conditions of Definition
2.4
as well.
∎

If (
6.2
) additionally holds with interchanged roles of
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
, then we obtain equality of warped coorbit spaces.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\colon D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\kappa:D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be such that
(\Phi_{1},\kappa)
and
(\Phi_{2},\kappa)
form compatible pairs as per Definition
2.4
, and suppose that there is a constant
C>0
, such that

(6.5)

\|D\Phi_{3-j}(\Phi_{j}^{-1}(\tau))\cdot D\Phi_{j}^{-1}(\tau)\|\leq C,\quad\text{for all }\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }j\in\{1,2\}.

Then we have, for all
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, the equality

(6.6)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{1},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{2},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}).

Proof.

By Theorem
4.16
we have, for arbitrary
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
and
j\in\{1,2\}
, the equalities

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{j},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{j}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell^{q}_{u_{j}}),\quad\text{with }(u_{j})_{\ell}=\kappa(\Phi_{j}^{-1}(\delta\ell))\cdot[w_{j}(\delta\ell)]^{1/q-1/2},\ \ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

Since (
6.5
) holds, we can apply Lemma
6.3
as is and with interchanged roles of
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
, to obtain equivalence of the coverings
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{2}}
. By Theorem
2.13
, we obtain the desired equality (
6.6
), provided that
u_{1}\asymp u_{2}
. Lemma
6.3
further implies that

\kappa(\Phi_{1}^{-1}(\delta k))\asymp\kappa(\Phi_{2}^{-1}(\delta\ell)),\quad\text{for all }k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\ \text{with }Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell}\neq\varnothing.

It remains to show that, likewise,

w_{1}(\delta k)\asymp w_{2}(\delta\ell),\quad\text{for all }k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\ \text{with }Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell}\neq\varnothing.

To do so, first note that, for any
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
and
j\in\{1,2\}
, we can estimate the Lebesgue measure
\mu
of
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{j},k}
a follows:

(6.7)

\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{j},k})=\int_{D}{\mathds{1}}_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{j},k}}(\xi)~d\xi=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\det(D\Phi_{j}^{-1})(\tau)|\cdot{\mathds{1}}_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{j},k}}(\Phi_{j}^{-1}(\tau))~d\tau=\int_{\delta\cdot B_{r}(k)}w_{j}(\tau)~d\tau\asymp w_{j}(\delta k),

for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Here, we used that
w_{j}
is
v_{j}
-moderate, with respect to the control weight
v_{j}
for the
(d+1)
-admissible warping function
\Phi_{j}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
, such that the implied constants in (
6.7
) depend on
\delta
,
r
,
d
, and
v_{j}
. Now let
R=(C+1)r
be as in the proof of Lemma
6.3
and
R_{2}=(C+1)R
, with
C>0
as in (
6.5
). Then, as in the proof of said Lemma,
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell}\neq\varnothing
implies
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k}\subset Q^{(\delta,R)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell}
and, likewise,
Q^{(\delta,R)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell}\subset Q^{(\delta,R_{2})}_{\Phi_{1},k}
. By an application of (
6.7
) with
j=1
, we thus find that

w_{1}(\delta k)\lesssim\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1},k})\leq\mu(Q^{(\delta,R)}_{\Phi_{2},\ell})\leq\mu(Q^{(\delta,R_{2})}_{\Phi_{1},k})\lesssim w_{1}(\delta k).

Applying (
6.7
) again with
j=2
, we obtain
w_{1}(\delta k)\asymp w_{2}(\delta\ell)
as desired. Therefore, we can apply Theorem
4.16
to show the desired equality (
6.6
) of warped coorbit spaces.
∎

As we now show, the assumptions of both results above simplify greatly in the case of radial warping functions
\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
,
\Phi_{2}=\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
generated from the the radial components
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}
.

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be
0
-admissible radial components, such that there is a constant
C>0
with

(6.8)

(\varrho_{2})^{\prime}(\xi)\leq C(\varrho_{1})^{\prime}(\xi),\quad\text{for all }\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

then
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
satisfy the conditions of Lemma
6.3
. In particular, the covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{1},r_{1})}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta_{2},r_{2})}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
, for every
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
and
r_{1},r_{2}>\sqrt{d}/2
.

Proof.

By
[
19
, Lemma 8.4]
, we have for
\varrho\in\{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},(\varrho_{1})_{\ast},(\varrho_{2})_{\ast}\}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
that

D\Phi_{\varrho}(\xi)=\widetilde{\varrho}(|\xi|)\cdot\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}+\varrho^{\prime}(|\xi|)\cdot\pi_{\xi},

where
\pi_{\xi}
is the orthogonal projection onto the span of
\xi
, and
\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}-\pi_{\xi}
. It is easy to see that

(a\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}+b\pi_{\xi})\cdot(c\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}+d\pi_{\xi})=ac\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}+bd\pi_{\xi}\quad\text{and}\quad\|a\pi_{\xi}^{\perp}+b\pi_{\xi}\|=\max\{a,b\},

for all
a,b,c,d\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Using
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}=\Phi_{{\varrho_{1}}_{\ast}}
, we obtain for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}

\|D\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\tau))\cdot D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\tau)\|\leq\max\{\ (\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\tau|)\cdot(\varrho_{2})^{\prime}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|)),\ \widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{2}}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))\ \}.

By assumption,
(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\tau|)\cdot(\varrho_{2})^{\prime}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))\leq C(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\tau|)\cdot(\varrho_{1})^{\prime}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))=C
. Further, since
\varrho_{1}(0)=\varrho_{2}(0)=0
, Equation (
6.8
) implies
\varrho_{2}\leq C\varrho_{1}
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and thus

\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{2}}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))=\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\cdot\frac{\varrho_{2}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))}{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|)}\leq C\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\cdot\frac{\varrho_{1}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|))}{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(|\tau|)}=C.

By continuity, the estimate
\|D\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\tau))\cdot D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\tau)\|\leq C
extends from
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
to all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.6
.

Let
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be such that
(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\kappa)
and
(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\kappa)
form compatible pairs as per Definition
2.4
. In particular,
assume that
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}
are
(d+1)
-admissible radial components. If
\varrho_{1}^{\prime}\asymp\varrho_{2}^{\prime}
and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, then we have the equality

(6.9)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q}).

Proof.

Use Corollary
6.5
twice to verify (
6.5
) in the assumptions of Theorem
6.4
, setting
\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
\Phi_{2}=\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
. Then said theorem yields (
6.9
).
∎

Remark 6.7
.

As a consequence of Corollary
6.6
, we immediately obtain that different slow start versions
\varrho_{1},\ \varrho_{2}
of the same weakly
k
-admissible radial component
\varsigma
generate the same coorbit spaces.

If
\varrho_{1}^{\prime}
and
\varrho_{2}^{\prime}
are not proportional, i.e., (
6.8
)
does not hold
with the roles of
\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{2}
interchanged, then we require additional restrictions on
\varrho_{1}
to prove embeddings between the coorbit spaces associated with the radial warping functions
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
. The next result, concerned with relative moderateness of the induced
(\delta,r)
-frequency coverings, is the final step towards such embedding relations.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be
0
-admissible radial components and

\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}^{(\delta,r)}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}^{(\delta,r)}
the respective induced
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency coverings, for arbitrary
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
.
Then

(6.10)

1+|\xi|\asymp 1+(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\quad\forall\,j\in\{1,2\},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\,\text{ and }\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{j}},k}^{(\delta,r)}\,.

If, additionally, there exists a function
C_{\varrho_{1}}:[1,\infty)\rightarrow[1,\infty)
, such that

(6.11)

C_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)^{-1}\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\xi)\leq\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\alpha\xi)\leq C_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\xi),\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in[1,\infty),\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

then
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}^{(\delta,r)}
is relatively
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}^{(\delta,r)}
-moderate.

Specifically, if (
6.11
) holds, we have

(6.12)

\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)\asymp[\widetilde{\varrho_{1}}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))]^{-1}\quad\forall\,k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ with }Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,,

and

(6.13)
