w_{1}(\delta k)=\det(D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k))\asymp\frac{[\widetilde{\varrho_{1}}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))]^{1-d}}{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))}\quad\text{if}\quad Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,.

In particular,
w_{1}
is relatively
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}^{(\delta,r)}
-moderate.

Proof.

We first prove (
6.10
).
Fix
j\in\{1,2\}
and let
v_{j}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a control weight for
\varrho_{j}
.
For
\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{j}},k}^{(\delta,r)}=\Phi_{\varrho_{j}}^{-1}\big(\delta\cdot B_{r}(k)\big)
, we have

{\varrho_{j}}(|\xi|)=|\Phi_{\varrho_{j}}(\xi)|\in\delta|k|+[-\delta r,\delta r]
.
Since
{\varrho_{j}}
is strictly increasing, this implies

(6.14)

({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|-\delta r)\leq|\xi|\leq({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|+\delta r)\,.

Now, let us first consider the case
|k|\geq 2r+2
, so that

|k|\asymp|k|-r>0
and
|k|+r\asymp|k|
.
Since by definition of a
0
-admissible radial component,

\widetilde{({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}}
is
v
-moderate, we see

|\xi|\leq({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|+\delta r)=\widetilde{({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|+\delta r)\cdot(\delta|k|+\delta r)\lesssim v_{j}(\delta r)\cdot\widetilde{({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)\cdot\delta|k|=v_{j}(\delta r)\cdot(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\,,

for
\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{j}},k}^{(\delta,r)}
. The lower bound
[v_{j}(\delta r)]^{-1}({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\lesssim|\xi|
is derived similarly, such that overall
|\xi|\asymp({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)
, for
\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{j}},k}^{(\delta,r)}
, and thus, whenever
|k|\geq 2r+2
, we have
1+|\xi|\asymp 1+({\varrho_{j}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)
.

To prove (
6.10
), it remains
to consider the case
|k|\leq 2r+2
, for which Equation (
6.14
) yields

|\xi|\leq(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(\delta|k|+\delta r)\leq(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(3\delta r+2\delta)\lesssim 1\quad\text{and}\quad(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\leq(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(2\delta r+2\delta)\lesssim 1.

Together,
1+|\xi|\asymp 1\asymp 1+(\varrho_{j})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)
if
\xi\in Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}
and
|k|\leq 2r+2
, as desired.

To prove Equation (
6.12
), first note that
\varnothing\neq Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}^{(\delta,r)}\ni\xi
implies,
by (
6.10
), that
1+({\varrho_{1}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp 1+|\xi|\asymp 1+({\varrho_{2}})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)
, i.e., there exists
C_{0}\geq 1
, such that

C_{0}^{-1}\cdot(1+({\varrho_{2}})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))\leq 1+({\varrho_{1}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\leq C_{0}\cdot(1+({\varrho_{2}})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)).

Moreover, since
\varrho_{1}(0)=0
, (
6.11
) implies

(6.15)

\alpha C_{\varrho}(\alpha)^{-1}\varrho_{1}(\xi)\leq\varrho_{1}(\alpha\xi)\leq\alpha C_{\varrho}(\alpha)\varrho_{1}(\xi),\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in[1,\infty),\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{+}.

If
k=0
or
\ell=0
, then Equations (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) are trivial: Both sides are positive and there are only finitely many
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},0}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing
. Likewise, there are only finitely many
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},0}^{(\delta,r)}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing
.
Hence, we can assume that
k\neq 0\neq\ell
. Then, (
6.10
) implies
({\varrho_{1}})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp({\varrho_{2}})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)
, and we obtain

[\widetilde{\varrho_{1}}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))]^{-1}=\frac{(\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)}{\varrho_{1}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))}\asymp\frac{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)}{\delta|k|}=\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|),

where we used (
6.15
) and
(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp(\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)
to deduce
\varrho_{1}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))\asymp\varrho_{1}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\delta|k|))=\delta|k|
.

To prove the last part of the proposition, recall from (
5.5
) that
\det(D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k))=(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)\cdot[\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)]^{d-1}
and recall that it suffices to consider the case
k\neq 0\neq l
. Because of
(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp(\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|)
we get

(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)=[\varrho_{1}^{\prime}((\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\delta|k|))]^{-1}\asymp[\varrho_{1}^{\prime}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))]^{-1},

by an application of (
6.11
). Estimating the remaining term
[\widetilde{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)]^{d-1}
with (
6.12
) yields the desired estimate (
6.13
).

With (
6.13
), we can derive that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is relatively
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderate. To see this, recall from Lemma
3.2
that the elements
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}
of the semi-structured coverning
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
can be written as
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}=T_{k}Q_{k}^{\prime}+b_{k}
, with
T_{k}=D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k)
, and hence
\det(T_{k})=w_{1}(\delta k)
, such that (
6.13
) yields relative
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderateness.
∎

We are now ready to prove embeddings
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{1}}^{p,q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{2}}^{p,q})
, for appropriate weights
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}
.

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be such that
(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\kappa)
and
(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\kappa)
form compatible pairs as per Definition
2.4
. In particular,
assume that
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}
are
(d+1)
-admissible radial components. If there is a constant
C>0
, and a function
C_{\varrho_{1}}:[1,\infty)\rightarrow[1,\infty)
, such that

(\varrho_{2})^{\prime}(\xi)\leq C(\varrho_{1})^{\prime}(\xi),\quad\text{and}\quad C_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)^{-1}\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\xi)\leq\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\alpha\xi)\leq C_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(\xi),\quad\text{for all }\alpha\in[1,\infty),\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

then, for all
p,q\in[1,\infty]
, we have the embeddings

(6.16)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},-\tilde{t}}}^{p,q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}}^{p,q}),\

with
\tilde{t}=\max(0,\max(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})-q^{-1})\leq 1
, and
t=\max(0,q^{-1}-\min(p^{-1},1-p^{-1}))\leq 1
. Here, for
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}:=\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}^{(q)}:=\kappa\cdot\left[\frac{w_{2}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}{w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}-t}\quad\text{and}\quad w_{j}=\det(D\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho_{j}}),\quad j\in\{1,2\}.

Proof.

We begin by showing that
(\Phi_{1},\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t})
forms a compatible pair in the sense of Definition
2.4
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
. By assumption,
(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\kappa)
form a compatible pair, and
w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}=w_{1}
is
v_{1}
-moderate, with
v_{1}
a (submultiplicative) control weight for the
(d+1)
-admissible warping function
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
. Further, it is a direct consequence of the definition of admissible warping functions, Definition
2.2
, that
(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},w_{2}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{2}})
form a compatible pair. By Corollary
6.5
, we can apply Lemma
6.3
, which yields compatibility of
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
w_{2}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
. Altogether, we conclude that
\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}
is moderate with respect to the weight

\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\cdot\left[v_{2}(C\bullet)\cdot v_{1}\right]^{\left|\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}-t\right|},

where
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}
is the moderating weight for the compatible pair
(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\kappa)
, see Definition
2.4
,
v_{2}
is a submultiplicative, radially increasing control weight for
\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
, see Definition
2.2
, and the proof of Lemma
6.3
shows that
w_{2}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}
is moderate with respect to
v_{2}(C\bullet)
.

Hence, by Theorem
4.16
we can identify
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}}^{p,q})
and
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
with decomposition spaces. Specifically, we obtain, for all
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
,

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell^{q}_{u_{2}}),\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}}^{p,q})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell^{q}_{u_{1}^{(t)}}),

with

(u_{2})_{\ell}=\kappa(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}^{-1}(\delta\ell))\cdot[w_{2}(\delta\ell)]^{1/q-1/2}\ \ \text{and}\ \ (u_{1}^{(t)})_{k}=\kappa(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k))\cdot\left[w_{1}(\delta k)\right]^{t}\cdot\left[w_{2}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k)))\right]^{1/q-1/2-t},

for all
\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. For ease of notation, and without loss of generality, we will subsequently choose the same values of
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
for both coverings.

In order to apply Theorem
6.2
to prove the desired embeddings, it remains to verify that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
, and that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
and
u_{1}^{(t)}
are relatively
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderate. The first property follows directly from Corollary
6.5
, and an application of Proposition
6.8
yields relative
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderateness of
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
.

To show that
u_{1}:=u_{1}^{(t)}
is relatively
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderate,
note as a consequence of Lemma
6.3
(applied with
\kappa
and with
w_{2}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}
instead of
\kappa
) that if
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}\neq\varnothing
, then

(6.17)

\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho_{1}}(\delta k))\asymp\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho_{2}}(\delta\ell))\quad\text{and}\quad w_{2}(\delta\ell)\asymp w_{2}((\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k)).

Finally, Equation (
6.13
) in Proposition
6.8
shows that
w_{1}(\delta k)=\det(D\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k))\asymp\frac{[\widetilde{\varrho_{1}}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))]^{1-d}}{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}((\varrho_{2})_{\ast}(\delta|\ell|))}
. This clearly shows that
u_{1}^{(t)}
is relatively
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
-moderate.

We have now verified all the prerequisites for applying Theorem
6.2
, Items (1)
and (2), with
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
,
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}

and the weights
v=u_{1}^{(t)}
,
u=u_{2}
. To apply Theorem
6.2
(1), we set
t=\max(0,q^{-1}-\min(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})
.
Select, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, some arbitrary
k_{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},k_{\ell}}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\ell}\neq\varnothing
. Then, recalling the form of
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}}
as semi-structured coverings, given in Lemma
3.2
,
K_{t}
in Theorem
6.2
(1) takes the form

\begin{split}K_{t}&=\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left|\frac{(u_{1}^{(t)})_{k_{\ell}}}{(u_{2})_{\ell}}\cdot[w_{2}(\delta{\ell})]^{t}\cdot[w_{1}(\delta k_{\ell})]^{-t}\right|\\
&=\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left|\frac{\kappa(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k_{\ell}))}{\kappa(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}^{-1}(\delta\ell))}\right|\cdot\left[\frac{w_{1}(\delta k_{\ell})}{w_{1}(\delta k_{\ell})}\right]^{t}\cdot\left[\frac{w_{2}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta k_{\ell})))}{w_{2}(\delta{\ell})}\right]^{1/q+1/2-t}.\end{split}

The second factor above trivially equals
1
. The other two factors are bounded by a constant, see Equation (
6.17
). In total, we obtain
K_{t}<\infty
. Since
K_{t}
is finite, we obtain the embedding
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},t}}^{p,q})
. The second embedding is proven analogously, with an application of Theorem
6.2
(2). Specifically, we set
t=-\tilde{t}=-\max(0,\max(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})-q^{-1})
and obtain

K_{\tilde{t}}=\sup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left|\frac{(u_{2})_{\ell}}{(u_{1}^{(-\tilde{t})})_{k_{\ell}}}\cdot[w_{2}(\delta{\ell})]^{\tilde{t}}\cdot[w_{1}(\delta k_{\ell})]^{-\tilde{t}}\right|<\infty,

such that Theorem
6.2
(2) yields
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},-\tilde{t}}}^{p,q})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{2}},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

Note that, in in the context of Theorem
6.9
, the seemingly weaker assumption that
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism and a
(d+1)
-admissible warping function is, in fact, equivalent to
(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\kappa)
being a compatible pair: Under the conditions of the theorem, we can apply Lemma
6.3
, which then yields compatibility of
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
\kappa
.

7.
Embeddings between Besov spaces and warped coorbit spaces

The homogeneous and inhomogeneous Besov spaces
[
1
,
34
]
, although they have historically been defined in various ways, are prototypical decomposition spaces. Specifically, they can be described as decomposition spaces with respect to a radial, dyadic covering and exponential weights. In particular, for the
inhomogeneous
Besov spaces
B^{p,q}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have the equality
B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{v^{s}}^{q})
, with the weight
v^{s}=(v^{s}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
,
v^{s}_{j}=2^{js}
and the
inhomogeneous Besov covering

\mathcal{B}=(B_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, given by

(7.1)

B_{0}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,|\xi|<2\}\quad\text{and}\quad B_{j}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,2^{j-1}<|\xi|<2^{j+1}\}\quad\text{for }j\in\mathbb{N}\,,

see also
[
14
, Definition 2.2.1]
. In particular,
v^{s}
is
\mathcal{B}
-moderate and the covering
\mathcal{B}
is semi-structured and tight
[
36
, Lemma 9.10]
.

We now proceed to investigate how warped coorbit spaces relate to inhomogeneous Besov spaces. In particular, we show that, for any admissible radial component
\varrho
, the
\Phi_{\varrho}
-induced
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering is almost subordinate to the standard inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}
just defined. However, we also show that the converse can only be true in dimension
d=1
, even if
\Phi
is not a radial warping function. Nonetheless, we obtain general conditions for embeddings between
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})
and
B_{s}^{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for specific weights
\kappa
depending on
s
. Specifically, if
\varrho_{1}
is any slow start version of the weakly admissible radial component
\varsigma_{1}=\ln(1+\bullet)
, we show equality of
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
and
B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
in dimension
d=1
, for a certain weight
\kappa
. In dimension
d\geq 2
, we obtain a
closeness
relation, in the sense that
B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
can be nested between two warped coorbit spaces with respect to
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
that only differ by a minor modification of the weight
\kappa
.

The following result shows that the covering
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
induced by
\Phi_{\varrho}
is almost subordinate to, and even relatively moderate with respect to, the inhomogeneous Besov covering.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
0
-admissible radial component and let
\mathcal{B}
be the inhomogeneous Besov covering.
Then the
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}

induced by
\Phi_{\varrho}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{B}
,
for arbitrary
\delta>0
,
r>\sqrt{d}/2
.
Specifically, we have the properties

(7.2)

\Big|j-\log_{2}\big(1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\big)\Big|\lesssim 1\quad\text{if}\quad B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,,

as well as

(7.3)

\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)\asymp\frac{2^{j}}{\varrho(2^{j})}\quad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\text{ and }k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ with }B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,.

Furthermore, if
\varrho
is
C^{2}
, and there are
R,C>0
such that

(7.4)

\left|\frac{\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\right|\leq C<\infty\quad\forall\,\gamma\in[R,\infty)\,,

then
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is relatively
\mathcal{B}
-moderate, with

(7.5)

w(\delta k)\asymp\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}\big(\varrho(2^{j})\big)\cdot\left(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\right)^{d-1}\quad\text{whenever }j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\text{ and }k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ with }B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,.

Remark 7.2
.

If Condition (
7.4
) is satisfied,
and if the weight
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is given by

\kappa(\xi)=(1+|\xi|)^{s}
for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then
the associated weight
u^{(q)}
defined in (
4.2
)
is relatively
\mathcal{B}
-moderate. Indeed, if
\varnothing\neq B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\ni\xi
,
then
1+|\xi|\asymp 2^{j}
,
so that Equation (
6.10
) shows

\kappa\big(\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}(\delta k)\big)=\kappa\big(\Phi_{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta k)\big)=(1+|\Phi_{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta k)|)^{s}=\big(1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\big)^{s}\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{s}\asymp 2^{sj}\,.

In combination with Equation (
7.5
),
we thus see

(7.6)

u_{k}^{(q)}=\kappa\big(\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}(\delta k)\big)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\asymp 2^{sj}\cdot\left[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}\big(\varrho(2^{j})\big)\cdot\left(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\right)^{d-1}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\text{if }B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\,,

which implies in particular that
u^{(q)}
is relatively
\mathcal{B}
-moderate.

Proof.

Let
v:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a control weight for
\varrho
.
To prove Equation (
7.2
),
fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and let
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be arbitrary. If
\varnothing\neq B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\ni\xi
, then
1+|\xi|\asymp 2^{j}
follows directly from the definition of
B_{j}
. In combination with (
6.10
), we thus see
that there are
0<c_{0}\leq 1\leq c_{1}
(independent of
j,k,\xi
) such
that
c_{0}\cdot\big(1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\big)\leq 2^{j}\leq c_{1}\cdot\big(1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\big)
. Hence,

\big|j-\log_{2}(1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|))\big|\leq\max\{\log_{2}(c_{1}),\log_{2}(c_{0}^{-1})\}
, such that
(
7.2
) is satisfied.

In particular, there are no more than
1+2\max\{\log_{2}(c_{1}),\log_{2}(c_{0}^{-1})\}

nonnegative integers
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
which satisfy this estimate. We thus see that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,:\,B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\}|\leq 1+2\max\{\log_{2}(c_{1}),\log_{2}(c_{0}^{-1})\}<\infty\,,

and
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{B}
.
Since the elements of
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
are path-connected, Lemma
2.14
shows that
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{B}
.

Our proof of Equation (
7.3
) distinguishes two cases: Since
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{B}
, the set

\{(j,k)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,:\,|k|\leq 2r\text{ and }B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\}
is finite. Since
\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}
and
2^{\bullet}\big/\varrho(2^{\bullet})
are positive functions,

\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)\asymp 2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})
for all
(j,k)
in the above set. Note that Equation (
6.14
) implies that the set
\{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\,:\,B_{0}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing\}
is likewise finite, yielding
\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)\asymp 2^{0}\big/\varrho(2^{0})
for all such
k
.

Finally, we consider the case
|k|>2r
and
j\neq 0
. To this end, note that the map
h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},\tau\mapsto\ln(\varrho(e^{\tau}))

is well-defined and strictly increasing, since
\varrho
is strictly increasing
with
\varrho(0)=0
. Setting
\gamma:=\varrho(e^{\tau})>0
, we see that the derivative
of
h
satisfies

0\leq h^{\prime}(\tau)=\frac{1}{\varrho(e^{\tau})}\cdot\varrho^{\prime}(e^{\tau})\cdot e^{\tau}=\frac{1}{\gamma}\cdot\frac{1}{\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)}\cdot\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)=\frac{\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\gamma)}{\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)}\leq C_{0}^{-1}\,,

where we used
\varrho^{\prime}=[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}\circ\varrho]^{-1}
by the inverse function theorem and, since
\varrho
is
0
-admissible, that
\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)\geq C_{0}\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\gamma)
, cf. Equation (
5.3
). The above estimate implies in particular that

|h(\tau)-h(\sigma)|\leq C_{0}^{-1}\cdot|\tau-\sigma|

for all
\tau,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let
\xi\in B_{j}\cap Q^{\delta,r}_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}
and set
\tau:=\ln(|\xi|)
and
\sigma:=\ln(2^{j})
, such that

|\tau-\sigma|\leq\ln(2)
, since
2^{-1}\leq|\xi|/2^{j}\leq 2
.
Therefore,

(7.7)

C_{0}^{-1}\ln(2)\geq|h(\tau)-h(\sigma)|=\big|\ln\big(\varrho(|\xi|)\big)-\ln\big(\varrho(2^{j})\big)\big|=\ln\bigg(\max\bigg\{\frac{\varrho(|\xi|)}{\varrho(2^{j})},\frac{\varrho(2^{j})}{\varrho(|\xi|)}\bigg\}\bigg)\,,

and hence
\varrho(2^{j})\asymp\varrho(|\xi|)\asymp\delta|k|
, where the latter equivalence follows from Equation (
6.14
), in the proof of Proposition
6.8
, with
|k|\geq 2r
.
Since
k,j\neq 0
and
|\xi|\geq 2^{j-1}\geq 1
, we have
1+\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)
and

1+|\xi|\asymp|\xi|
. Therefore, Equation (
6.10
) implies
\varrho_{\ast}(\delta|k|)\asymp|\xi|\asymp 2^{j}
. Together with the prior estimate, we obtain (
7.3
).

To prove the last part of the proposition, let us assume that
(
7.4
) is satisfied.
Note that
\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}
is a
positive
, continuous function,
since
\varrho
is a strictly increasing diffeomorphism.
Therefore, the left-hand side of
(
7.4
) is a continuous function,
so that (
7.4
) is in fact
satisfied for all
\gamma\in[0,\infty)
,
after possibly enlarging the constant
C
.
In the following, we will assume that
C
is chosen in this way.

Now, define
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},t\mapsto\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(t))

and
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},\tau\mapsto\ln(f(e^{\tau}))
.
Setting
\gamma:=\varrho(e^{\tau})>0
,
we obtain, with (
7.4
),

|g^{\prime}(\tau)|=\left|\frac{1}{f(e^{\tau})}\cdot f^{\prime}(e^{\tau})\cdot e^{\tau}\right|=\left|\frac{e^{\tau}\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\varrho(e^{\tau}))\cdot\varrho^{\prime}(e^{\tau})}{\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(e^{\tau}))}\right|=\left|\frac{\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\right|\leq C<\infty,\quad\text{for all }\tau\in\mathbb{R},

where we used
\varrho^{\prime}=[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}\circ\varrho]^{-1}
. We conclude that

(7.8)

|g(\tau)-g(\sigma)|\leq C\cdot|\tau-\sigma|,\quad\text{for all }\tau,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}.

Now, let
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
satisfy

\varnothing\neq B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\ni\xi
,
and recall from (
6.14
) that

\big|\,\varrho(|\xi|)-\delta|k|\,\big|\leq\delta r
.
Since
\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}
is
v
-moderate, and since
v
is radially increasing,
this implies

\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)=\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}\big(\delta|k|-\varrho(|\xi|)+\varrho(|\xi|)\big)\leq v(\delta r)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(|\xi|))=v(\delta r)\cdot f(|\xi|)\,.

Likewise,
f(|\xi|)\leq v(\delta r)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)
. Now,

f(|\xi|)\asymp f(2^{j})
and consequently
\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)\asymp\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))
, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\varnothing\neq B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}

is shown analogous to (
7.7
), with an application of (
7.8
).
Combining this with Equations (
5.5
) and
(
7.3
), we thus see

w(\delta k)=\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\delta|k|)\cdot[\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\delta|k|)]^{d-1}\asymp\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))\cdot\left(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\right)^{d-1}

for
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with

B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing
,
proving (
7.5
)
for positive
j\in\mathbb{N}
. However, if
j=0
, then we recall that there are only finitely many
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
B_{0}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing
. Since both sides of (
7.5
) are positive, equivalence is trivial.
∎

We just saw that the covering
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is always
almost subordinate to the inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}
.
Our next result provides a straightforward, sufficient condition for the converse to be true, but only in the
one-dimensional case
d=1
. In Theorem
7.10
, we will construct an example of an admissible radial component which satisfies this condition. The situation is radically different in higher dimensions: In Theorem
7.4
below, we show that
\mathcal{B}
is never weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}^{(\delta,r)}
, if
d\geq 2
. This holds regardless of
\Phi
being radial or not.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
d=1
, let
r>1/2
,
let
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
0
-admissible radial component,
and assume that
\varrho
is
C^{2}
and satisfies
Equation (
7.4
).
If

(7.9)

2^{j}\lesssim\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))=[\varrho^{\prime}(2^{j})]^{-1}\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,,

then the inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}=(B_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
defined
in (
7.1
) is weakly subordinate to

\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
.

Proof.

Proposition
7.1
shows that there is
some
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
there is some
j_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
satisfying

Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\subset B_{j_{k}^{N\ast}}
.
For
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, set

I_{j}:=\big\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\cap B_{j}\neq\varnothing\big\}\,.

Now, if
k\in I_{j}
, then

\varnothing\neq B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\subset B_{j}\cap B_{j_{k}^{N\ast}}
,
and hence
j_{k}\in j^{(N+1)\ast}
,
which implies
j_{k}^{N\ast}\subset j^{(2N+1)\ast}
, and finally

(7.10)

Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\subset B_{j_{k}^{N\ast}}\subset B_{j^{(2N+1)\ast}}\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\text{ and }k\in I_{j}\,.

Since
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is an admissible covering,
there is some
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathds{1}}_{Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}}\leq K
.
In view of Equation (
7.10
),
this shows
\sum_{k\in I_{j}}{\mathds{1}}_{Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}}\leq K\cdot{\mathds{1}}_{B_{j^{(2N+1)\ast}}}
.
Thus, noting that the factor
(2^{j}/\varrho(2^{j}))^{d-1}
in Equation (
7.5
) vanishes for
d=1
, we obtain

\begin{split}|I_{j}|\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))&\overset{\eqref{eq:RadialWarpingCoveringRelativeModerateness}}{\asymp}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}w(\delta k)\overset{\text{ Lem. }\ref{lem:DecompInducedCoveringIsNice}}{\asymp}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}\mu\big(Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\big)\\
&\overset{\text{(MCT)}}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}{\mathds{1}}_{Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}}(\xi)\,d\xi\leq K\cdot\mu\big(B_{j^{(2N+1)\ast}}\big)\lesssim 2^{j}\,,\end{split}

where (MCT) indicates an application of the monotone convergence theorem. Now,
apply (
7.9
) to show that
\mathcal{B}
is weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
. Specifially, we obtain
|I_{j}|\lesssim 2^{j}\big/\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))\lesssim 1
.
∎

The next result shows that Lemma
7.3
cannot be extended to dimensions
d\geq 2
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
2\leq d\in\mathbb{N}
,
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open, and
\Phi\colon D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
a diffeomorphism. The
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
induced by
\Phi
, see Definition
1.1
, is not weakly equivalent to
\mathcal{B}
, for any choice of
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
.

Before we prove Theorem
7.4
, we require some auxiliary results, estimating the number of
n
-th order neighbors for
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
and
\mathcal{B}
, see also Remark
2.8
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
2\leq d\in\mathbb{N}
,
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open, and
\Phi\colon D\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
a diffeomorphism. Then, for any
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, the
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}=(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi,k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
induced by
\Phi
satisfies

(7.11)

k^{n\ast}\supset\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}~:~\|k-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leq n\},\quad\text{such that }|k^{n\ast}|\geq(1+2n)^{d},\quad\text{for all }k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Moreover, the inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}=(B_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfies

(7.12)

j^{n\ast}\subset\{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}~:~|j-\ell|\leq n\},\quad\text{such that }|j^{n\ast}|\leq 1+2n,\quad\text{for all }j,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Proof.

We first prove the upper bound for
|j^{n\ast}|
, i.e., Equation (
7.12
). If
\xi\in B_{j}\cap B_{\ell}\neq\varnothing
, with
j,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, then
2^{\ell-1}<|\xi|<2^{j+1}
, and thus
\ell-1<j+1
. implying
\ell\leq j+1
. Repeat the argument with interchanged roles of
j,\ell
to obtain
|j-\ell|\leq 1
. Further, if
j=0
instead, then
2^{\ell-1}<|\xi|<2=2^{1}
, similarly yielding
|0-\ell|\leq 1
. Overall,

j^{\ast}\subset\{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}~:~|j-\ell|\leq 1\},\quad\text{such that }|j^{\ast}|\leq 3,\quad\text{for all }j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

Equation (
7.12
) now follows by a straightforward induction.

The proof of Equation (
7.11
) is slightly more involved. Note that
r>\sqrt{d}/2
implies

P_{k}\subset B_{r}(k),\quad\text{where}\quad P_{k}=k+[-1/2,1/2]^{d}.

Therefore
P_{k}\cap P_{\ell}\neq\varnothing
implies
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi,k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi,\ell}=\Phi^{-1}(\delta\cdot(B_{r}(k)\cap B_{r}(\ell)))\neq\varnothing
. Hence, it suffices to prove (
7.11
) for
\mathcal{P}=(P_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
in place of
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
.

Now define, for
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\varepsilon:=k-\ell
and note that
\|k-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leq 1
implies
\varepsilon\in[-1,1]^{d}
. In that case, we clearly have
k-\varepsilon/2=\ell+\varepsilon/2\in P_{k}\cap P_{\ell}
. Hence,

\ell\in k^{\ast},\quad\text{whenever }k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ with }\|k-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leq 1.

Finally, assume that the first part of Equation (
7.11
) holds for some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\|k-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}=n+1
. There exists a
\tilde{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\|k-\tilde{k}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leq n
and
\|\tilde{k}-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}=1
. Hence,
\tilde{k}\in k^{n\ast}
and
\ell\in\tilde{k}^{\ast}
, such that
\ell\in k^{(n+1)\ast}
follows. This proves the first part of Equation (
7.11
) by induction. To prove the second part, simply note that
|\{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}~:~\|k-\ell\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leq n\}|=(1+2n)^{d}
.
∎

Our second auxiliary result provides an estimate for neighbor sets of arbitrary order, for two equivalent coverings.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=(Q_{i})_{i\in I}
and
\mathcal{P}=(P_{j})_{j\in J}
be two equivalent, admissible coverings of a common set
\mathcal{O}
, such that
Q_{i}
,
i\in I
, are nonempty. Then there are constants
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(7.13)

|i^{n\ast}|\leq M\cdot|j^{N(n+1)\ast}|,

for all
i\in I,j\in J
such that
Q_{i}\cap P_{j}\neq\varnothing
, and arbitrary
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Proof.

Recall that
Q_{L}
, for some
L\subset I
, denotes the set
Q_{L}:=\bigcup_{i\in L}Q_{i}
and
P_{L}
for some
L\subset J
is defined analogously. Since
\mathcal{Q}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{P}
, there exists, by
[
36
, Lemma 2.11 (3)]
, an
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
Q_{i}\subset P_{j^{N\ast}}
, for all
i\in I,j\in J
such that
Q_{i}\cap P_{j}\neq\varnothing
. A straightforward induction shows that, in fact,
Q_{i^{n\ast}}\subset P_{j^{N(n+1)\ast}}
holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, whenever
Q_{i}\cap P_{j}\neq\varnothing
. Denote by
I_{j}
,
j\in J
, the sets

I_{j}=\{i\in I~:~Q_{i}\cap P_{j}\neq\varnothing\}.

Since
\mathcal{P}
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}
, it is, in particular, weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}
(
[
36
, Lemma 2.11 (1)]
, see also Remark
2.12
), such that
M:=\sup_{j\in J}|I_{j}|<\infty
.

Now, let
i\in I
and
j\in J
be arbitrary, with
P_{j}\cap Q_{i}\neq\varnothing
. Since
Q_{i^{n\ast}}\subset P_{j^{N(n+1)\ast}}
holds, we also have
Q_{k}\subset P_{j^{N(n+1)\ast}}
for all
k\in i^{n\ast}
, i.e., there is some
\ell\in j^{N(n+1)\ast}
with
Q_{k}\cap P_{\ell}\neq\varnothing
. We conclude that
i^{n\ast}\subset\bigcup_{\ell\in j^{N(n+1)\ast}}I_{\ell}
. Therefore,

|i^{n\ast}|\leq\Big|\bigcup_{\ell\in j^{N(n+1)\ast}}I_{\ell}\Big|\leq\sum_{\ell\in j^{N(n+1)\ast}}|I_{\ell}|\leq M\cdot|j^{N(n+1)\ast}|.\hfill\qed

Proof of Theorem
7.4
.

If
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
are weakly equivalent, then they are equivalent, since their elements are path-connected non-empty. Applying Lemma
7.6
with
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{B}
and inserting the estimates in Lemma
7.5
,
we obtain, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

(1+2n)^{d}\leq|k^{n\ast}|\leq M\cdot|j^{N(n+1)\ast}|\leq M\cdot(1+2N(n+1)),

for some constants
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
, and all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi,k}\cap B_{j}\neq\varnothing
. Since
d\geq 2
, this is a contradiction (let
n\rightarrow\infty
).
∎

As a consequence of Theorem
7.4
, we obtain the following negative result on equality between warped coorbit spaces and Besov spaces.

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
, and let
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\kappa\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
be compatible in the sense of Definition
2.4
. If
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{1},q_{1}}_{\kappa})
coincides, up to canonical identifications, with an (inhomogeneous) Besov space
B^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then
p_{1}=p_{2}=q_{1}=q_{2}=2
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.16
, we have

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}.q_{1}}_{\kappa})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell^{q_{1}}_{u}),

where
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}=(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi,k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
, with arbitrary
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
, is the
\Phi
-induced
(\delta,r)
-fine frequency covering and
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is a suitable
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
-moderate weight.
Hence, the assumptions of the theorem imply

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell^{q_{1}}_{u})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi,\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}.q_{1}}_{\kappa})=B^{p_{2},q_{2}}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B},\mathbf{L}^{p_{2}},\ell^{q_{2}}_{v}),

where
v=(2^{js})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Since
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi}
and
\mathcal{B}
are not weakly equivalent by Theorem
7.4
,
[
36
, Theorem 6.9, Items (1) and (3)]
shows that
p_{1}=p_{2}=2
. Finally,
[
36
, Theorem 6.9, Item (4)]
shows that
q_{1}=q_{2}=2
and the proof is finished.
∎

Now that we have determined the limits of identifying warped coorbit spaces with Besov spaces, we return to what is possible in the setting of radial warping functions
\Phi_{\varrho}
. The next lemma is the final step for preparing our embedding result, stated in Proposition
7.9
below. We show that the weight
\kappa(\xi)=(1+|\xi|)^{s}
, together with any radial warping function
\Phi_{\varrho}
derived from a
0
-admissible radial component
\varrho
, form a compatible pair, see Definition
2.4
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a
0
-admissible radial component with control weight
v\colon\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
and let
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{s}

for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, such that
\kappa\circ\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}
is
\tilde{\kappa}
-moderate, with
\tilde{\kappa}=\left(1+C\cdot(1+|\bullet|)\cdot v(\bullet)\right)^{|s|}
. In particular, if
\varrho\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is
(d+1)
-admissible, then
\Phi_{\varrho}
and
\kappa
are compatible in the sense of Definition
2.4
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to show the result for
\kappa,\tilde{\kappa}
with
s=1
, since all relevant
properties are retained if real powers
s\in\mathbb{R}
of the moderate weight, respectively nonnegative powers
|s|

of the moderating weight are taken.

Let
v:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
denote a control weight for the radial component

\varrho
. In particular, this implies that
\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}
is

v
-moderate, see Definition
5.2
.
We will show that
\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}=\kappa\circ\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}
is
\tilde{\kappa}
-moderate for the weight

\tilde{\kappa}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\ \xi\mapsto 1+C\cdot(1+|\xi|)\cdot v(|\xi|)\,,\quad\text{with}\quad C:=\max\{2,\max_{\tau_{0}\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\tau_{0})\},

which is easily seen to be radially increasing, continuous,
and submultiplicative.

Observe that
\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\xi)=1+|\Phi_{\varrho_{\ast}}(\xi)|=1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\xi|)
, for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Since
\varrho_{\ast}
is increasing, and since
\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}
is

v
-moderate, we have

\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\xi+\tau)=1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\xi+\tau|)\leq 1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\xi|+|\tau|)=1+\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(|\xi|+|\tau|)\cdot(|\xi|+|\tau|)\leq 1+\big(v(|\xi|)\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\big)\cdot(|\xi|+|\tau|)=:\circledast\,.

We now distinguish three cases:

Case 1:
If
|\tau|\leq 1
, then

\circledast\leq 1+\max_{\tau_{0}\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(\tau_{0})\cdot(1+|\xi|)\cdot v(|\xi|)\leq\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\leq\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot(1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|))=\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\tau)\,.

Case 2:
If
|\xi|\leq|\tau|
and
|\tau|>1
, then

\circledast\leq 1+v(|\xi|)\cdot\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(|\tau|)\cdot 2|\tau|=1+2v(|\xi|)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|)\leq\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot(1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|))=\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\tau)\,.

Case 3:
If
1<|\tau|\leq|\xi|
, then

\widetilde{\varrho_{\ast}}(|\tau|)=\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|)/|\tau|\leq\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|)
, and

\circledast\leq 1+2|\xi|\cdot v(|\xi|)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|)\leq\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot(1+\varrho_{\ast}(|\tau|))=\tilde{\kappa}(\xi)\cdot\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\tau)\,.\qed

Finally, we can characterize the existence of embeddings of the warped
coorbit spaces
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p,q})
, with the
weight
\kappa=(1+|\bullet|)^{s}
into inhomogeneous Besov spaces, and vice versa.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
and a
(d+1)
-admissible radial component, with

\left|\tfrac{\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\right|\leq C<\infty\quad\text{for some }R>0\ \text{and}\ \gamma\in[R,\infty)\,.

Furthermore, let
\kappa:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}

for some
s_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Then the warped coorbit spaces

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})

are well-defined and independent of the choice
of
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\setminus\{0\}
,
for arbitrary
p_{1},q_{1}\in[1,\infty]
. We further have the following equivalences, for
p_{1},p_{2}\in[1,\infty]
:

(1)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})\hookrightarrow B_{s_{2}}^{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds if and only if

(7.14)

p_{1}\leq p_{2}\quad\text{and }\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{2}-s_{1}+d\tilde{t})}\cdot\left[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))\cdot\big(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\big)^{d-1}\right]^{p_{1}^{-1}-p_{2}^{-1}-\tilde{t}+2^{-1}-q_{1}^{-1}}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{2}\cdot(q_{1}/q_{2})^{\prime}}}<\infty\,,

where
\tilde{t}=\max(0,\max(p_{2}^{-1},1-p_{2}^{-1})-q_{1}^{-1})
.

(2)

B_{s_{2}}^{p_{2},q_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})

holds if and only if

(7.15)

p_{2}\leq p_{1}\quad\text{and }\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{1}-s_{2}+dt)}\cdot\left[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))\cdot\big(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\big)^{d-1}\right]^{p_{2}^{-1}-p_{1}^{-1}-t+q_{1}^{-1}-2^{-1}}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{q_{1}\cdot(q_{2}/q_{1})^{\prime}}}<\infty\,,

where
t:=\max(0,q_{1}^{-1}-\min(p_{2}^{-1},1-p_{2}^{-1}))
.

Proof.

By
[
36
, Lemma 9.10]
,
\mathcal{B}
is a tight semi-structured covering of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Further, Theorem
5.4
shows that
\Phi_{\varrho}
is a
(d+1)
-admissible warping function, such that Theorem
2.5
shows that the warped coorbit spaces

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})
are well-defined and independent of the choice of
0\neq\theta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Lemmas
3.2
and
3.5
show that
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}
is semi-structured and tight. Finally, by Lemma
7.8
, the tuple
(\Phi_{\varrho},\kappa)
is a compatible pair as per Definition
2.4
, such that the assumptions of Theorem
4.16
are satisfied, and

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho},\mathbf{L}_{\kappa}^{p_{1},q_{1}})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)},\mathbf{L}^{p_{1}},\ell_{u^{(q_{1})}}^{q_{1}})
, with
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
arbitrary, up to canonical identifications. The weight
u^{(q_{1})}
is defined as in (
4.2
), i.e.,

u_{k}^{(q_{1})}=\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho}}(\delta k)\cdot[w(\delta k)]^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}}.

Since, by assumption, there exist
C,R>0
, such that
\left|\tfrac{\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\right|\leq C
, for all
\gamma\in[R,\infty)
, Proposition
7.1
shows that
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}^{(\delta,r)}

is almost subordinate to, and relatively moderate with respect to,
\mathcal{B}
and, as discussed in Remark
7.2
,
u^{(q_{1})}
is relatively
\mathcal{B}
-moderate as well. Hence, the assumptions of Theorem
6.2
, Items (1) and (2), are satisfied.

Finally, together with Lemma
3.2
, which specifies that the operator
T_{k}
in the semi-structured representation of
Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}
is given by
T_{k}=D\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}(\delta k)
, we obtain

|\det T_{k}|=w(\delta k)\asymp\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho(2^{j}))\cdot\big(2^{j}\big/\varrho(2^{j})\big)^{d-1}=:\gamma_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad u_{k}^{(q_{1})}\asymp 2^{s_{1}j}\cdot\gamma_{j}^{q_{1}^{-1}-2^{-1}}\,,

if
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
satisfy

B_{j}\cap Q_{\Phi_{\varrho},k}^{(\delta,r)}\neq\varnothing
. The equivalences (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) are now direct
consequences of Theorem
6.2
, Items (2) and (1), respectively.
∎

Closing the discussion of Besov embeddings, we show that for
d=1
, and choosing the radial warping function derived from a certain weakly
k
-admissible radial component,
B^{p,q}_{s}(\mathbb{R})
is recovered as a warped coorbit space. For
d\geq 2
,
B^{p,q}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is nested between two warped coorbit spaces which only differ by a minimal change of the weight
\kappa
.

Theorem 7.10
.

The function
\varsigma_{1}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty),\xi\mapsto\ln(1+\xi)
is a weakly
k
-admissible radial component for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Assume that
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and let
\varrho_{1}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be any
slow-start
version of
\varsigma_{1}
, see Theorem
5.6
. Further, define for
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
d\in\mathbb{N}
,

\kappa^{(s,q)}:=\kappa^{(d,s,q)}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\quad\xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{s+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}.

We have

(7.16)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(s+\varepsilon,q)}})\hookrightarrow B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(s-\varepsilon,q)}})\qquad\forall\,\varepsilon>0\,.

If and only if
d=1
or
p=q=2
, the equality

(7.17)

B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(s,q)}})\,

holds. If
d\geq 2
and
p\neq 2
, then neither of the two embeddings implied by (
7.17
) is valid. If
d\geq 2
, and
p=2
, then
B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(s,q)}})
if and only if
q\leq 2
and, likewise,
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(s,q)}})\hookrightarrow B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
q\geq 2
.

Proof.

It was already shown in
[
19
, Example 8.16]
that
\varsigma_{1}
is weakly
k
-admissible, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Hence, we conclude that
\Phi_{\varrho}
is
(d+1)
-admissible for any
d\in\mathbb{N}
, by Theorems
5.6
and
5.4
. Before we can apply Proposition
7.9
, it remains to show that there exist
C,R>0
, such that
\left|\tfrac{\varrho_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[\varrho_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\right|\leq C
, for all
\gamma\in[R,\infty)
. However, by construction, see Theorem
5.6
, there is some
R>0
such that
\varrho_{1}(\xi)=\varsigma_{1}(\xi)
for all
\xi\in[R,\infty)
and, possibly after increasing
R
,
(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\xi)=(\varsigma_{1})_{\ast}(\xi)=e^{\xi}-1
as well.
Consequently, we obtain the desired bound

\Big|\frac{(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}(\gamma)\cdot(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)}{[(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\gamma)]^{2}}\Big|=\frac{(e^{\gamma}-1)e^{\gamma}}{e^{2\gamma}}\leq 1\qquad\forall\,\gamma\in[R,\infty)\,.

Given that
(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)=e^{|\xi|}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-R,R)
, a short calculation show that

(7.18)

2^{j}\asymp(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho_{1}(2^{j}))\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,.

We are now ready to apply Proposition
7.9
, where due to the chosen weight
\kappa^{(s,q)}
we have
s_{1}+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1})
in place of
s_{1}
. By Equation (
7.18
), and additionally noting that
\varrho_{1}(2^{j})\asymp\varsigma_{1}(2^{j})\asymp 1+j
,
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we can rewrite the norm expression in Equation (
7.14
), with
p_{1}=p_{2}=p
and
q_{1}=q_{2}=q
, as

(7.19)

\begin{split}K_{\tilde{t}}&=\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{2}-(s_{1}+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1}))+d\tilde{t})}\cdot\left[(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho_{1}(2^{j}))\cdot\big(2^{j}\big/\varrho_{1}(2^{j})\big)^{d-1}\right]^{2^{-1}-q^{-1}-\tilde{t}}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\\
&\asymp\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{2}-s_{1})}\big/(1+j)^{(d-1)(-\tilde{t}+2^{-1}-q^{-1})}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}}=:(*),\end{split}

with
\tilde{t}=\max(0,\max(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})-q^{-1})
. Similarly,
the norm expression in Equation (
7.15
) simplifies to

(7.20)

\begin{split}K_{t}&=\left\|\left(2^{j[(s_{1}+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1}))-s_{2}+dt]}\cdot\left[(\varrho_{1})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\varrho_{1}(2^{j}))\cdot\big(2^{j}\big/\varrho_{1}(2^{j})\big)^{d-1}\right]^{q^{-1}-2^{-1}-t}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\\
&\asymp\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{1}-s_{2})}\big/(1+j)^{(d-1)(q^{-1}-2^{-1}-t)}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}}=:(\star),\end{split}

with
t:=\max(0,q^{-1}-\min(p^{-1},1-p^{-1}))
. In the setting of this Theorem, we consider

s_{2}=s
and
s_{1}\in\{s-\varepsilon,s,s+\varepsilon\}
. It is easily seen that
(\ast)<\infty
if
s=s_{2}<s_{1}=s+\varepsilon
and likewise
(\star)<\infty
if
s=s_{2}>s_{1}=s-\varepsilon
, such that the closeness relation (
7.16
) follows from Proposition
7.9
.

If
s_{1}=s_{2}=s
, then

(\ast)=\left\|\left((1+j)^{(d-1)(\tilde{t}-2^{-1}+q^{-1})}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}},\quad\text{and}\quad(\star)=\left\|\left((1+j)^{(d-1)(t-q^{-1}+2^{-1})}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}}

which are finite if and only if
0\geq(d-1)(\tilde{t}-2^{-1}+q^{-1})
, respectively
0\geq(d-1)(t-q^{-1}+2^{-1})
. Both inequalities are trivially satisfied if
d=1
, such that the equality (
7.17
) holds. If
d>1
, then
d-1>0
, but a simple check of all possible configurations reveals that
\tilde{t}-2^{-1}+q^{-1}
is positive, unless
p=2
and
q\geq 2
, when it equals
0
. Likewise,
t-q^{-1}+2^{-1}>0
, unless
p=2
and
q\leq 2
, when it equals
0
. Hence, Proposition
7.9
yields the equality (
7.17
) for
d=1
and
p=q=2
. The above discussion also settles the remaining cases for
d\geq 2
, as stated in the theorem. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.11
.

It is easy to see that the statement of Theorem
7.10
remains true, if we substitute
\kappa=\kappa^{s,q}
given in the theorem by

\kappa^{(1,s,q)}:=\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\ \xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{s+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\xi|)|)^{(1-d)(2^{-1}-q^{-1})},

a weight that we will encounter again in our study of embeddings between warped coorbit spaces and
\alpha
-modulation spaces in Section
8
.

To see that
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
\kappa^{(1,s,q)}
are compatible, recall that
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
(1+|\bullet|)^{s}
are compatible by Lemma
7.8
, and that
(1+|\bullet|)^{d(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\bullet|)|)^{(1-d)(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}
is just
[w\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}]^{2^{-1}-q^{-1}}
, which is clearly compatible with
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
, in disguise:
By construction of
\varrho_{1}
, we have, for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{2\varepsilon}(0)
,
|\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}(\xi)|=\ln(1+|\xi|)
. Furthermore, with
\delta=\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}(2\varepsilon)>0
, we have, for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\delta}(0)
,that
w(\tau)=e^{d|\tau|}\cdot|\tau|^{1-d}\asymp e^{d|\tau|}\cdot(1+|\tau|)^{1-d}
. Hence,
w(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}(\xi))\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{d}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\bullet|)|)^{(1-d)}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{2\varepsilon}(0)
. On the other hand, if
\xi\in\overline{B_{2\varepsilon}(0)}
, then
w(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}(\xi))\asymp 1\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{d}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\bullet|)|)^{(1-d)}
, such that both weights are equivalent. In particular,
(1+|\bullet|)^{d}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\bullet|)|)^{(1-d)}
is
Cv_{0}^{d}
-moderate, where
C\geq 1
and
v_{0}
is a control weight for the
k
-admissible warping function
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
.

Now, by exactly the same steps as in the proof of Theorem
7.10
, the expressions (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) are equivalent to the even simpler

\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{2}-s_{1})}\cdot(1+j)^{(d-1)\tilde{t}}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}},\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\left(2^{j(s_{1}-s_{2})}\cdot(1+j)^{(d-1)t}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\|_{\ell^{\infty}},\quad\text{respectively.}

These conditions are, in turn, satisfied for the exact same values of
s_{1},\ s_{2}
and
\tilde{t},\ t
as the analogous conditions given in (
7.19
) and (
7.20
).

Remark 7.12
.

Note that it can be shown that shearlet-type coverings as discussed in

[
23
,
39
]

are weakly subordinate to the covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}
.
In fact, the
connected
shearlet covering
(see
[
39
, Definition 3.1]
)
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}
and also relatively
moderate with respect to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho}}
.
Hence, one can also completely characterize the embedding relations between

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\rho},L^{p,q}_{\kappa_{\gamma}})

and the shearlet smoothness spaces discussed in

[
23
,
39
]
. We leave the detailed derivation to the interested reader.

We will now turn our attention to
\alpha
-modulation spaces
and (Besov-type)
spaces of dominating mixed smoothness

[
27
,
28
,
40
]
, showing that these spaces coincide with certain warped coorbit spaces. In either case, we note that the discretization results in
[
19
]
imply that warped time-frequency systems provide atomic decompositions for the respective spaces that differ from previous constructions, see, e.g.,
[
2
,
33
,
6
]
for
\alpha
-modulation spaces and
[
40
, Section 2.4]
for spaces of dominating mixed smoothness.

8.
Warped coorbit spaces as (
\alpha
-)modulation spaces

The
\alpha
-modulation spaces
[
15
,
2
]
are a family of decomposition spaces defined with respect to coverings that are in some sense geometrically intermediate between the uniform covering and the dyadic, (inhomogeneous) Besov covering. We will show that the radial warping obtained from
\varsigma_{\beta}(t)=(1+t)^{1/\beta}-1
for
\beta\geq 1
recovers

\alpha
-modulation spaces with
\alpha=1-\beta^{-1}\in[0,1)
. The following definition is a slightly extended version of
[
2
, Definitions 2.1 and 2.4]
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=(Q_{i})_{i\in I}
be an admissible covering of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
and let
\alpha\in(-\infty,1]
.
The family
\mathcal{Q}
is called an
\alpha
-covering of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, if the
following hold:

(1)

With

(8.1)

\quad\quad\quad r_{Q}:=\sup\{r>0\,:\,\exists\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:B_{r}(\xi)\subset Q\}\quad\!\!\text{and}\quad\!\!R_{Q}:=\inf\{R>0\,:\,\exists\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:Q\subset B_{R}(\xi)\}\,,

we have

\sup_{i\in I}R_{Q_{i}}/r_{Q_{i}}<\infty
.

(2)

We have
\mu(Q_{i})\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{\alpha\cdot d}

for all
i\in I
and
\xi\in Q_{i}
,
where the implied constant does not depend on
i,\xi
,
and where
\mu
denotes the Lebesgue measure as usual.

If
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
,
\mathcal{Q}^{(\alpha)}
is an
\alpha
-covering, and
u^{s}=(u^{s}_{i})_{i\in I}
, with
u^{s}_{i}=[\mu(Q_{i}^{(\alpha)})]^{s(d\alpha)^{-1}}
for some
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then the
\alpha
-modulation space

M^{s,\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is defined by

(8.2)

M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\alpha)},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u^{s}}^{q}).

Remark 8.2
.

In Appendix
A
, we show that the resulting function space is independent of the choice of the
\alpha
-covering
\mathcal{Q}^{(\alpha)}
, see also
[
2
, Lemma B.2]
.

Note that prior work
[
15
,
2
]
was concerned specifically with the case
\alpha\in[0,1]
. We show in Appendix
A
that
\alpha\in(-\infty,1]
is indeed the most general setting, and the natural generalization of Defintion
8.1
to
\alpha>1
yields an empty set of
\alpha
-coverings for any such
\alpha
. To justfiy the notion of
\alpha
-coverings,
\alpha\in[0,1]
, as
intermediate
between the uniform and dyadic coverings, consider Item (
2
) in Definition
8.1
and observe that the uniform covering
\mathcal{Q}=(Q+k)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
with a
suitable base set
Q
satisfies

\mu(Q+k)\asymp 1=(1+|\xi|)^{0\cdot d}

for
\xi\in Q+k
, whereas the dyadic covering
\mathcal{B}
consisting of the annuli

B_{j}=B_{2^{j+1}}(0)\setminus\overline{B_{2^{j-1}}(0)}
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
and a ball
B_{0}=B_{2}(0)
, satisfies

\mu(B_{j})\asymp 2^{dj}\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{1\cdot d}
for

\xi\in B_{j}
.

In the following, we will consider warping functions
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
,
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
, derived from a slow start version of

(8.3)

\varsigma_{\alpha}\colon[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty),\quad\xi\mapsto(1+\xi)^{1-\alpha}-1.

A straightforward derivation shows that
\varsigma_{\alpha}
is indeed a weakly
k
-admissible radial component, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Hence, any slow start version
\varrho_{\alpha}
of
\varsigma_{\alpha}
, generates a
k
-admissible warping function
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
, see Theorem
5.6
and the ensuing remark. We now proceed to show that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
is an
\alpha
-covering, such that
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u^{s}}^{q})=M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for an appropriate choice of
\kappa=\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
and
\varrho_{\alpha}
be an arbitrary slow start version of
\varsigma_{\alpha}
, as in Theorem
5.6
. Then, for any
\delta>0
and
r\geq\sqrt{d}/2
,
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
is an
\alpha
-covering.

Proof.

Since
\varrho_{\alpha}
is a slow start version of
\varsigma_{\alpha}
, there is a
R>0
, such that with
\beta=(1-\alpha)^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we have

(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}(\xi)=(\varsigma_{\alpha})_{\ast}(\xi)=(1+\xi)^{\beta}-1\quad\text{and}\quad(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)=\beta\cdot(1+\xi)^{\beta-1},\quad\text{for all }\xi\geq R,

as well as
\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}\asymp 1\asymp(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}
in
[0,R)
. Using
\xi\asymp 1+\xi
for
\xi>R
, we see that in fact

\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}(\xi)\asymp(1+\xi)^{\beta-1}\asymp(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(\xi)

on all of
[0,\infty)
and with the trivial equality
\frac{x^{p}}{y^{p}}=\frac{y^{-p}}{x^{-p}}
for
x,y,p\in\mathbb{R}
, we find that

v\,\colon\,\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\ \xi\mapsto C\cdot(1+|\xi|)^{|\beta-1|},

is a control weight for
\varrho_{\alpha}
as per Definition
5.2
, for an appropriate constant
C>0
depending on
d
and
\beta
.

In order to show that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
satisfies Definition
8.1
(
1
), note that

\begin{split}\left|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)-\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega)\right|&=\left|\int_{0}^{1}D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega+s(\xi-\omega))\langle\xi-\omega\rangle~ds\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega+s(\xi-\omega))\langle\xi-\omega\rangle\right|~ds.\end{split}

By
[
19
, Lemma 8.4]
, we have

D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega+s(\xi-\omega))=\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)\cdot\pi^{\bot}_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}+(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)\cdot\pi_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}

In particular, an application of Parseval’s identity for orthonormal bases yields

\begin{split}\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\left|D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega+s(\xi-\omega))\langle\xi-\omega\rangle\right|^{2}$\mbox{}\hfil\\
&=|\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)\cdot\pi^{\bot}_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}(\xi-\omega)|^{2}+|(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)\cdot\pi_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}(\xi-\omega)|^{2}\\
&\asymp(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)^{2}\left(|\pi^{\bot}_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}(\xi-\omega)|^{2}+|\pi_{\omega+s(\xi-\omega)}(\xi-\omega)|^{2}\right)\\
&=|(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)|^{2}\cdot|\xi-\omega|^{2},\end{split}

where we used
(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}\asymp\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}
and
(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}>0
.
Altogether, we obtain

\left|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)-\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\omega)\right|\asymp\int_{0}^{1}|(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\omega+s(\xi-\omega)|)|\cdot|\xi-\omega|~ds.

Now set
\omega=\delta k
and let
\xi\in\delta\cdot(\overline{B_{r}(k)}\setminus B_{r}(k))
, i.e.,
|\delta k-\xi|=\delta r
. Since
s(\omega-\xi)\in\overline{B_{\delta r}(0)}
, and
(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}
is
v
-moderate with radially increasing weight
v
, we further have

[v(\delta r)]^{-1}\cdot(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\delta k|)\leq(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\delta k+s(\xi-\delta k)|)\leq v(\delta r)\cdot(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\delta k|),

such that

\left|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)-\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\delta k)\right|\asymp(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\delta k|)\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{\beta-1}.

We conclude that
R_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}
and
r_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}
,
defined as in Equation (
8.1
) satisfy

(8.4)

R_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}\asymp r_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{\beta-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}R_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}/r_{Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}}<\infty.

It remains to prove that
\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{d\alpha}
, for all
\tau\in Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}
. For
\tau=\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\delta k)
, we have
|\delta k|=|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}(\tau)|\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{1/\beta}-1
, such that

(1+|\delta k|)^{\beta-1}\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{\alpha}.

With Equation (
8.4
), we conclude that
\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{d\alpha}
for this value of
\tau
. On the other hand, if
\tau=\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)
, with
\xi\in\delta\cdot B_{r}(\delta k)
, then by the same argument,

(1+|\tau|)^{(\beta-1)/\beta}\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{\beta-1}\overset{(\ast)}{\asymp}(1+|\delta k|)^{\beta-1},

where the proportionality constants for
(\ast)
can be chosen as
(1+\delta r)^{-|\beta-1|}
and
(1+\delta r)^{|\beta-1|}
, using that
(1+|\cdot|)^{\beta-1}
is
(1+|\cdot|)^{|\beta-1|}
-moderate. Overall,

\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})\asymp(1+|\tau|)^{d\alpha}
for all
\tau\in Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}
, completing the proof.
∎

We are now ready to show equality between
\alpha
-modulation spaces and certain coorbit spaces associated to
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
,
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and choose any slow start version
\varrho_{\alpha}
of
\varsigma_{\alpha}
. Denote
w_{\alpha}=\det(D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\bullet))
and define the weight
\kappa:=\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}\,\colon\,\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
by

(8.5)

\kappa:=\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}\,\colon\,\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,\infty),\ \xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{\gamma},\quad\text{with }\gamma=s-d\alpha(q^{-1}-2^{-1}).

Then, up to canonical identifications,

(8.6)

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})=M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\,,

where
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
, with
u_{k}=\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho_{\alpha}}(\delta k))\cdot[w_{\alpha}(\delta k)]^{q^{-1}-2^{-1}}
.

Proof.

In Lemma
8.3
, we have shown that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
is an
\alpha
-cover, such that the second equality in (
8.6
) follows from the definition of the
\alpha
-modulation space
M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as decomposition space if we show that

u_{k}=\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{\varrho_{\alpha}}(\delta k))\cdot[w_{\alpha}(\delta k)]^{q^{-1}-2^{-1}}\asymp[\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})]^{s(d\alpha)^{-1}}

as a function in
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. In the proof of Lemma
8.3
, we have shown that
\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{d(\beta-1)}\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{\frac{d\alpha}{1-\alpha}}
, with
\beta=(1-\alpha)^{-1}
. Furthermore,
\kappa(\Phi^{-1}_{\alpha}(\delta k))=(1+|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\delta k)|)^{\gamma}

and, by Theorem
5.4
,
w(\delta k)=(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}^{\prime}(|\delta k|)\cdot[\widetilde{(\varrho_{\alpha})_{\ast}}(|\delta k|)]^{d-1}\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{d(\beta-1)}=(1+|\delta k|)^{\frac{d\alpha}{1-\alpha}}
. Finally, since there exists some
R>0
, such that
(1+|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)|)^{\gamma}=(1+|\xi|)^{\gamma/(1-\alpha)}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{R}(0)
, we have, in fact
(1+|\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\xi)|)^{\gamma}\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{\gamma/(1-\alpha)}
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Putting all these estimates together, we obtain

u_{k}\asymp(1+|\delta k|)^{\frac{s}{1-\alpha}}=\left((1+|\delta k|)^{\frac{d\alpha}{1-\alpha}}\right)^{\frac{s}{d\alpha}}\asymp[\mu(Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k})]^{s(d\alpha)^{-1}}.

Hence, we have shown that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})=M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

To show the first equality in (
8.6
), we want to apply Theorem
4.16
. To do so, we only need to show that
(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}})
form a compatible pair as per Defintion
2.4
.
\Phi_{\alpha}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism and a
(d+1)
-admissible warping function by construction, see also Theorem
5.4
. As discussed above, the transplanted weight
\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
satisfies
\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}(\xi)\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{\gamma/(1-\alpha)}
, for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and is clearly moderate with respect to the continuous, radial and submultiplicative weight
\xi\mapsto C(1+|\xi|)^{|\gamma/(1-\alpha)|}
, for any sufficiently large
C\geq 1
. Hence,
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
and
\kappa_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
are compatible and Theorem
4.16
yields the desired equality
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa})=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{u}^{q})=M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
∎

After Definition
8.1
, we remarked the the inhomogeneous, dyadic Besov covering
\mathcal{B}
is an
\alpha
-covering for
\alpha=1
. However, the annulus-based geometry of
\mathcal{B}
is fundamentally different from the the coverings
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
,
\alpha\in[0,1)
, which consist of convex sets. Warped coorbit spaces provide an interesting alternative with radial warping based on the weakly
k
-admissible radial component
\varsigma_{1}=\ln(1+\bullet)
, which we have related to Besov spaces in the previous subsection. We obtain the following result.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
,
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and let
\varrho_{1}
be any slow start version of
\varsigma_{1}=\ln(1+\bullet)
. Define
\Phi_{1}:=\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and let
w_{1}:=\det(\mathrm{D}\Phi_{1}^{-1})
be the associated weight.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
and let
\tilde{T}:=\tilde{T}_{d,\alpha}:=\tilde{t}d(1-\alpha)
, with
\tilde{t}=-\max(0,\max(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})-q^{-1})
and
T:=T_{d,\alpha}:=td(1-\alpha)\geq 0
, with
t=\max(0,q^{-1}-\min(p^{-1},1-p^{-1}))
. Then we have the embedding

(8.7)

M_{p,q}^{s+\tilde{T},\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa})\hookrightarrow M_{p,q}^{s-T,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with the weight
\kappa:=\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}\,\colon\,\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
given by

(8.8)

\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}\,\colon\,\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,\infty),\ \xi\mapsto(1+|\xi|)^{s+d(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}\cdot(1+\ln(1+|\xi|))^{(1-d)(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}.

Proof.

Let, as before,
\varrho_{\alpha}
be a slow start version of
\varsigma_{\alpha}
, see Equation (
8.3
). Let further,

w_{\alpha}=\det(D\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}(\bullet))
denote the weight associated with
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
. Proposition
8.4
shows that
M_{p,q}^{s,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa})
, where
\kappa(\xi)=\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}(\xi)=(1+|\xi|)^{s+d\alpha(2^{-1}-q^{-1})}
is as in (
8.5
).
In particular,
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
and
\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}
are compatible. Compatibility of
\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}
and
\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}
was already proven in Remark
7.11
, where we also saw that
w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{d}(1+|1+|\ln(1+|\bullet|)|)^{1-d}
, such that
\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{s}\cdot[w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}]^{2^{-1}-q^{-1}}
. Similarly we can show that
w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{d\alpha}
and thus
\kappa^{(\alpha,s,d,q)}\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{s}\cdot[w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}]^{2^{-1}-q^{-1}}
.

Moreover, with the above derivations, and setting
\kappa=\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}
, we can already derive the following equivalence, which holds for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and will be used later in the proof.

(8.9)

\begin{split}\kappa\cdot\left[\frac{w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}{w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}\right]^{q^{-1}-2^{-1}-t}&\asymp(1+|\bullet|)^{s}\cdot[w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}]^{-t}\cdot[w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}]^{t+2^{-1}-{q}^{-1}}\\
&\asymp(1+|\xi|)^{s+td(\alpha-1)}\cdot[w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}]^{{2}^{-1}-{q}^{-1}}\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\xi||)^{t(d-1)}\\
&\asymp\kappa^{(\alpha,s-td(1-\alpha),d,q)}(\xi)\cdot(1+|\ln(1+|\xi|)|)^{t(d-1)}.\end{split}

We will prove (
8.7
) by means of Theorem
6.9
. Hence, we must first show that its assumptions are met. By Definition of
\varrho_{\alpha},\ \varrho_{1}
, there exists some
R>0
, such that
(\varrho_{\alpha})^{\prime}(\xi)=(1-\alpha)(1+|\xi|)^{-\alpha}
and
(\varrho_{1})^{\prime}(\xi)=(1+|\xi|)^{-1}
holds for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[-R,R]
. The existence of
C_{\alpha}>0
, such that
(\varrho_{1})^{\prime}(\xi)\leq C_{\alpha}(\varrho_{\alpha})^{\prime}(\xi)
for all such
\xi
is clear. After possibly increasing
C_{\alpha}
, this inequality extends to all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, since
[-R,R]
is compact and
(\varrho_{1})^{\prime},(\varrho_{\alpha})^{\prime}>0
are continuous. In particular, we can use Corollary
6.5
to see that the conditions of Lemma
6.3
are satisfied, such that
\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}
and
\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}
are compatible. Finally, we have, for all
a\in[1,\infty)
,
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\alpha\in[0,1)
, that
(1+|a\xi|)^{-\alpha}\leq(1+|\xi|)^{-\alpha}\leq a^{\alpha}\cdot(a(1+|\xi|))^{-\alpha}\leq C(a)(1+|a\xi|))^{-\alpha}
, with
C(a)<\infty
. For
\alpha\in(-\infty,0)
, analogous steps yield a similar proportionality relation. Hence, all assumptions of Theorem
6.9
are met, with
\kappa=\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}
.

Note that
t=\max(0,q^{-1}-\min(p^{-1},1-p^{-1}))\in[0,1]
, such that Equation (
8.9
) yields, with
T:=T_{d,\alpha}:=td(1-\alpha)\geq 0
,

\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},t}=\kappa\cdot\left[\frac{w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}{w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}-t}\gtrsim\kappa^{(\alpha,s-T,d,q)}.

Likewise, if we set
t=-\tilde{t}=\max(0,\max(p^{-1},1-p^{-1})-q^{-1})\in[-1,0]
and
\tilde{T}:=\tilde{T}_{d,\alpha}:=\tilde{t}d(1-\alpha)\geq 0
, then Equation (
8.9
) yields

\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},-\tilde{t}}=\kappa\cdot\left[\frac{w_{1}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}{w_{\alpha}\circ\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}+\tilde{t}}\lesssim\kappa^{(\alpha,s+\tilde{T},d,q)}.

The discussion in Section
6.1
, in particular Equation (
6.1
), shows that the estimates above imply

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(\alpha,s+\tilde{T},d,q)}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},-\tilde{t}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},t}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(\alpha,s-T,d,q)}}).

Moreover, by Theorem
6.9
, we obtain

\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},-\tilde{t}}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa_{\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{1},t}}),

and Proposition
8.4
yields
M_{p,q}^{s+\tilde{T},\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(\alpha,s+\tilde{T},d,q)}})
and
\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(\alpha,s-T,d,q)}})=M_{p,q}^{s-T,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Overall, we have shown that

M_{p,q}^{s+\tilde{T},\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Co}(\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa^{(1,s,d,q)}})\hookrightarrow M_{p,q}^{s-T,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

as desired.

∎

Remark 8.6
.

Combining our results from this section and Section
7
, we have shown that any
\alpha
-covering with
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
is almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
, which is in turn almost subordinate to the inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}
, which also is an
\alpha
-covering for
\alpha=1
. In dimension
d=1
,
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
and
\mathcal{B}
are weakly equivalent, but for
d>1
, Theorem
7.4
shows that
\mathcal{B}
is never almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
, i.e., it is not an
\alpha
-covering for
\alpha=1
. Likewise, it can be shown that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is not almost subordinate to any
\alpha
-covering with
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
, in particular, it is not an
\alpha
-covering at all.

To see this, fix some
\delta>0
and
r>\sqrt{d}/2
and denote by
K_{\ell}=\{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}~:~Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},k}\cap Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},\ell}\neq\varnothing\}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Now, consider
Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},ne_{1}}
, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and the first standard unit vector
e_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For any sufficiently large
n
, we have

Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}},ne_{1}}=\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}^{-1}(\delta\cdot B_{r}(ne_{1}))\supset\{ce_{1}~:~c+1\in e^{|\delta\cdot B_{r}(ne_{1})|}\}=\{ce_{1}~:~c+1\in e^{(\delta(n-r),\delta(n+r))}\}.

Furthermore, for any sufficiently large
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

Q^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}},me_{1}}\ni\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}^{-1}(\delta me_{1})=((1+\delta m)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}-1)e_{1}.

Hence, if
(1+\delta m)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\in e^{(\delta(n-r),\delta(n+r))}
, or equivalently
\ln(1+\delta m)\in(1-\alpha)\cdot(\delta(n-r),\delta(n+r))
, then
me_{1}\in K_{ne_{1}}
. In other words, for any sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\{m\in\mathbb{N}~:~\ln(1+\delta m)\in(1-\alpha)\cdot(\delta(n-r),\delta(n+r))\}\subset K_{ne_{1}}
, but clearly

|\{m\in\mathbb{N}~:~\ln(1+\delta m)\in(1-\alpha)\cdot(\delta(n-r),\delta(n+r))\}|\overset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty.

Hence,
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is not weakly subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
. Since the elements of both coverings are path-connected, it is thus not almost subordinate to
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{\alpha}}}
. Since all
\alpha
-coverings are equivalent and
\alpha\in(-\infty,1)
was arbitrary, we conclude that
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{\varrho_{1}}}
is not almost subordinate to any
\alpha
-covering.

Overall, it seems natural to consider
\mathcal{Q}^{(\delta,r)}_{\Phi_{1}}
as an intermediate between
\alpha
-coverings with
\alpha\rightarrow 1
and the inhomogeneous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}
. Interestingly, the embedding relations in Proposition
8.5
exactly mirror those obtained between
\alpha
-modulation and Besov spaces: Specifically, with
\tilde{T}=\tilde{T}_{d,\alpha}
and
T:=T_{d,\alpha}
as in the proposition, we obtain by
[
36
, Theorem 9.13, Lemma 9.15, Corollary 9.16]
,

M_{p,q}^{s+\tilde{T},\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow B_{s}^{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow M_{p,q}^{s-T,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

9.
Warped coorbit spaces as spaces of dominating mixed smoothness

In Section
7
, we have seen that—at least in dimension
d=1
—the radial warping generated from
\varsigma=\log(1+|\cdot|)
induces a
frequency covering equivalent to the inhomogeneous Besov covering, so that the
associated coorbit spaces are inhomogeneous Besov spaces.
In the following, we will show, for arbitrary dimension
d\in\mathbb{N}
, that certain warped coorbit spaces obtained by
d
-dimensional
tensorization
of a
1
-dimensional warping function, i.e.,
\Psi=\Psi_{\varrho}\otimes\ldots\otimes\Psi_{\varrho}
(where
\Phi_{\varrho}
is symmetric and thus radial), are
identical to (Besov-type)
spaces of dominating mixed smoothness

[
27
,
28
,
40
]
. The latter are defined as follows.

Definition 9.1

(Definition 1.2
[
40
]
)
.

Let
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and choose a
\mathcal{B}
-BAPU
\{\varphi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
for the
1
-dimensional, inhomogenous Besov covering
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, such that additionally, there are
c_{k}>0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with

(9.1)

2^{j}k\cdot|\varphi_{j}^{(k)}(t)|\leq c_{k},\quad\text{for all }j,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ t\in\mathbb{R}.

Define
\mathcal{B}_{d}=\{B_{d,\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
, where
B_{d,\ell}=B_{\ell_{1}}\times\ldots\times B_{\ell_{d}}
, and
v^{(s)}=\{v^{(s)}_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
, where
v^{(s)}_{\ell}=2^{\langle\ell,s\rangle}
. Then the space
S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is given by the collection

(9.2)

S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\left\{f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})~\colon~\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}_{d},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{v^{(s)}}^{q})}<\infty\right\},

equipped with the usual decomposition space norm
f\mapsto\|f\|_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}_{d},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{v^{(s)}}^{q})}
, defined with respect to the
\mathcal{B}_{d}
-BAPU
\{{\psi_{\ell}}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, where
\psi_{\ell}=\varphi_{\ell_{1}}(\bullet_{1})\cdot\ldots\cdot\varphi_{\ell_{d}}(\bullet_{d})
.

Remark 9.2
.

The decomposition space norm in Definition
9.1
was used only for notational convenience and it remains to be shown that
S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
does in fact coincide with
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}_{d},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{v^{(s)}}^{q})
. Strictly speaking, we also have to prove the claim in Definition
9.1
, that
\{{\psi_{\ell}}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
is a
\mathcal{B}_{d}
-BAPU. The required support and summation properties are, however, obvious. Observe further that (
9.1
) is equivalent to requiring that
\{\varphi_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is a regular partition of unity subordinate to
\mathcal{B}
in the sense of
[
36
, Definition 8.1]
, and as an immediate consequence,
\{{\psi_{\ell}}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
is a regular partition of unity subordinate to
\mathcal{B}_{d}
. Hence,
\{{\psi_{\ell}}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
is in fact a
\mathcal{B}_{d}
-BAPU (by
[
36
, Theorem 8.2]
) and
\mathcal{B}_{d}
a decomposition covering. Likewise, it is easy to see that
\mathcal{B}_{d}
-moderateness of
v^{(s)}
is implied by
\mathcal{B}
-moderateness of the
1
-dimensional Besov weight
v^{s}
with
v^{s}_{j}=2^{js}
.

It can be seen for
d=1
that the space
S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R})
of dominating mixed smoothness coincides with the inhomogeneous Besov space
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R})
. For larger values of
d
,
S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is, in some sense, an anisotropic alternative to Besov spaces, penalizing large values in the Fourier-domain with a weight that does not depend only on the modulus of the frequency position
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, but the modulus of its individual components
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{d}\in\mathbb{R}
.

We now proceed to prove that
S^{s}_{p,q}B(\mathbb{R}^{d})
coincides with the decomposition space
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}_{d},\mathbf{L}^{p},\ell_{v^{(s)}}^{q})
. For this purpose, we need an auxilliary result on the covering
\mathcal{B}_{d}
.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
\mathcal{B}=\{B_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
denote the
1
-dimensional, inhomogenous Besov covering, see (
7.1
). The covering
\mathcal{B}_{d}=\{B_{d,\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}
, where
B_{d,\ell}=B_{\ell_{1}}\times\ldots\times B_{\ell_{d}}
, of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is semi-structured with

B_{d,\ell}=S_{l}B_{d,\ell}^{\prime}\,,\quad\text{with}\quad S_{l}=\mathrm{diag}(2^{\ell_{1}},\ldots,2^{\ell_{d}})\quad\text{and}\quad B_{d,\ell}^{\prime}=B_{\ell_{1}}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times B_{\ell_{d}}^{\prime}.

Here,
B_{j}^{\prime}=(-2,-1/2)\cup(1/2,2)
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and
B_{0}^{\prime}=(-2,2)
. Furthermore, for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
, there exists some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that the weight
